# P1 — Guard / ExecutionHost (whole-phase report)

## 2026-10-26 — Process output-cap normalization regression

Parent review found that `Math.min(NaN, 128)` remains `NaN`. Added a test-first
regression in `packages/core/test/process-guard.test.ts`; the focused RED run
(`bun test test/process-guard.test.ts --test-name-pattern 'NaN output cap'`)
failed because output was not marked truncated. `packages/core/src/process.ts`
now normalizes each supplied output/error cap to an integer in `[0, fixedCap]`
before collection: NaN becomes zero, fractional values truncate toward zero,
and infinities/out-of-range values clamp to the corresponding bound. Omitted
caps use the fixed cap. Both `run` and `runStream` error capture use this
normalization, so `collectStream` never receives NaN or an unbounded default on
these paths.

The required full focused suite `cd packages/core && bun test
test/process-guard.test.ts` passed **9/9 tests, 25 expectations** after the fix. No Cargo, gate,
P2/P7/parent-owned files, or Git operations are in scope or were performed.

## 2. Compile repair completed (P1-owned files are clean)

`cargo check --lib --message-format=short` filtered to P1-owned files now returns **zero errors**. Start state was ~99 errors across the owned files (plus the shared-type drift below).

### `kernel/src/guard.rs`
- Destabilized `is_automation_definition_action` / `is_provider_inference_action` from `const fn` to `fn` (matching on `&str` in a `const fn` is unstable; callers are ordinary functions).
- Added `pub(crate) fn is_blake3_digest` (lowercase-hex + length, same shape as the existing `is_sha256_digest`) so process/journal modules stop duplicating ad-hoc digest checks.
- Updated the managed-scope test fixture for the new `ManagedAttemptScopeV1::permission_scope_digest` field.

### `kernel/src/effect_host.rs`
- Added one canonical `impl From<Failure> for TypedErrorV1` (delegates to `Failure::error`) so internal failures cross the typed RPC boundary with `?`; this removed ~15 scattered mapping failures without reclassifying any error.
- `Challenge` gained the durable fields the code already read/needed: `capability_lease_digest`, `direct_session_grant_digest`, `receipt_digest`. All five `effect_challenges` SELECTs feeding `row_to_challenge` now select them, and `row_to_challenge` maps indices 20–22.
- Fixed a malformed `Failure.Conflict.error()` (single-dot path) typo in the CodeMode prepare path.
- `stdio_executions` now persists `dap_allow_evaluate` (schema + migration row + INSERT + recovery SELECT/index + binding projection) instead of dropping the immutable DAP profile fact on recovery.
- Fixed `SQLITE` schema-string errors in `EffectHost::open` (String error type), `Option`/tuple annotation drift, `Result::filter`, `&&str`/`&String` mixes, borrow conflicts (`drop(stmt)` before `&mut self`), moved `receipt`, moved `row.8`, and stale tuple arities in cascade receipt checks.
- `http_execution`-facing behavior: receipt `receiptRef`/`receiptDigest` no longer move the same value.

### `kernel/src/terminal_process.rs`
- Uses the canonical `crate::guard::is_blake3_digest` (the local definition had been removed from `protocol.rs`), removed the test-only `TerminalProcess` initializer's missing Linux `cgroup` field, added `dap_allow_evaluate: false` to the two test bindings, and scoped the mutable-map borrows in the editor-profile test.
- **Full Access containment decision (DEC-V1-29 / HANDOFF 2026-10-22):** `prepare_full_access` no longer demands a retained cgroup (`cgroup_required: false`, `cgroup: None`) and its guarantee record no longer claims cgroup containment. The record is now the single constant `FULL_ACCESS_GUARANTEE_RECORD`: unsandboxed, supervised process-group lifetime only, no cgroup claim, restart without exact process-tree proof is UNKNOWN.
- **External editor containment:** `prepare_external_editor` only allocates/retains an exact cgroup for confined profiles; the explicit `danger-full-access + never` projection takes the supervised path with no retained cgroup.

### `horizoncode-sandbox/src/lib.rs`
- `GuaranteeDimension` derives `Ord`/`PartialOrd` (the approval projection uses it as a `BTreeMap` key and compares `GuaranteeDimension::ALL` order).

### `kernel/src/direct_turn_journal.rs` (contract: durable payload = `serde_json::Value`)
- All six journal append sites now project the wire value to `serde_json::Value` via `messagepack::Value::to_json_value()` (`durable_turn_scope`/`durable_attempt_scope`/`durable_authorization`).
- All seven replay decode sites convert the durable JSON back to the canonical wire shape with `protocol_framing::json_to_wire` (`wire_from_durable`) before calling the strict `from_value` decoders, so replay still validates the exact RPC shape.
- This removes the `messagepack::Value: Serialize` and `serde_json::Value` vs wire-type failures.

### `kernel/src/direct_message_journal.rs`
- Clone the receipt cursor digest before moving the cursor into `self.cursor`.

### `kernel/src/http_execution.rs`
- `reqwest::json` is unavailable (the crate deliberately disables that feature): the request body is now encoded once with `serde_json::to_vec` and sent as exact bytes with the existing `content-type: application/json` header.
- `canonical_search_input` returns owned `String` (it previously returned a reference into a local decoded value).
- Brave fixed-target validation passes `&query` to the bounded target builder.

### `kernel/src/workspace_command_profile.rs`
- Added the missing named lifetime to the strict `digest(...) -> Option<&str>` accessor.

### Test-only compile repair (`cargo check --tests` filter over P1 files)
- `cgroup_v2` test identity: added the new `delegated_root_owner_uid`, `cgroup_owner_uid`, `cgroup_hierarchy_hidden` fields.
- `effect_host` verifier fixtures: added the required `BrokerProcessAuthorizationV1.scope` (`Session`) and `dap_allow_evaluate: false` on four `StdioExecutionBindingV1` fixtures.
- `terminal_process`: `bind_stdio_execution_digest` now takes the execution principal; the test asserts the principal is part of the approval binding (a reviewer identity cannot substitute for the trusted process owner).

## 3. Full Access settlement (new source, fail-closed)

Removing the retained cgroup would have made Full Access settlement impossible (both terminal and external editor previously derived quiescence only from a cgroup identity). Added an explicit, labelled supervised-tree path:

- `terminal_process::FULL_ACCESS_GUARANTEE_RECORD` is the exact durable marker written into the HostKernel consent preflight for an authenticated Full Access launch.
- `EffectHost::require_terminal_process_quiescence` now accepts a `NULL` cgroup identity **only** when (a) the exact preflight record equals that marker and (b) the preflight pins a canonical direct permission-mode digest; it then mints `horizoncode.terminal.supervised-tree-quiescence.v1` evidence from the observed terminal status/exit code. Any other identity-less row returns `Unknown` (no replay).
- `settle_external_editor_execution` uses the durable `external_editor_runs.cgroup_identity` when present; a `None` identity is accepted only for `DirectSandboxModeV1::DangerFullAccess` (already pinned by the binding digest and `permission_mode_digest`) and mints `horizoncode.external-editor.supervised-tree-quiescence.v1` evidence. Cgroup kill/retain/remove is skipped for that path.
- No new HostKernel signature is required: the marker flows through the existing preflight record, and the editor mode flows through the existing authenticated binding.
- Cancel/unknown of a *prepared, never-spawned* Full Access editor still requires a cgroup identity and therefore resolves to UNKNOWN; this is fail-closed and listed below as remaining work.

## 4. Tests added

`kernel/src/effect_host.rs` → `mod supervised_quiescence_tests`:
- `supervised_tree_quiescence_is_deterministic_and_state_bound` — determinism, BLAKE3 shape, sensitivity to status/exit code, and terminal-vs-editor domain separation.
- `full_access_marker_is_exact_and_distinct_from_confined_launches` — the settlement gate is exact equality and the marker makes no cgroup/enforced claim.

These are `cfg(test)` and could not be executed yet because `cargo check --tests` requires the P2-owned library to compile. No test was disabled, deleted or weakened.

### Type-level repairs in `packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts`

None of these weaken Guard/lease/receipt semantics; several restore intended
fail-closed behavior that drift had broken.

- `directAttemptScopeFromWire` returned the unsigned-integer **wire** form while
  declaring the decimal-string Core form (a latent runtime defect: callers
  comparing/retaining the result would see bigints). It now validates through
  `directAttemptScopeToWire` and returns the string form, matching
  `directTurnScopeFromWire`.
- `AuthenticatedRequestContextV1.artifact_read`: `managedAttempt` is optional and
  `directAttempt` (wire form) is added. `validArtifactReadRequest` now requires
  **exactly one** Attempt lane (mutual exclusion, fail-closed) and validates the
  lane's session/location binding. This matches the kernel parser
  (`protocol.rs` `value_to_authenticated_context`, which rejects both-lanes).
- **Guard-relevant wire fix:** `HostKernelClientImpl.request` previously
  serialized every non-`repo_read` context as `kind: "artifact_read"`. The
  serializer now emits `artifact_scope_resolve`, `direct_session_artifact_read`,
  and `code_mode_call` faithfully per the kernel's strict parser, so the
  host-side authority class always matches the Core-side validation branch.
- `validateManagedVerifierChallenges` / `validateManagedRootGuardChallenge`
  build their returned challenge field-by-field with exact literal types
  (previously spread `...consent`, which mistyped `normalizedAction`,
  `allowEnabled`, and duplicated `origin`/`source`).
- Editor/REPL `prepare_user_effect` bodies keep the unsigned-integer wire scope
  (required by the Rust `DirectAttemptScopeV1::from_value` parser); the durable
  lease now echoes the caller's already-validated decimal-string Attempt scope,
  matching `EditorProtocolAttemptWireV1` and P4's `sameDirectAttemptScope`
  string comparisons. Both scopes were validated by the existing `*ScopeToWire`
  calls before use.
- `hostProcessRef.ownerEpoch` is re-extracted through a fail-closed
  `Effect.try` (u64 canonical form) in both the editor-protocol and REPL
  authorization paths instead of reading an `unknown`-typed record field.
- `Effect.catchAll` → `Effect.catch` (Effect v4 beta.83 API).
- Added `sameManagedAttemptScopeCore`: full-field equality over every managed
  Attempt scope field including the worktree target; invalid scopes compare as
  unequal (fail closed).
- `prepareToolBatch` input type (implementation and `Interface`) gained
  `workspaceCommandProfiles` with `resolvedEnvironment` — `pty.ts` already sent
  this; the validator already required it.
- `EffectAdmission` gained the `admitted_batch` member (mode-approved
  multi-call batch). P7's `tool-batch.ts:1596` already discriminates on this
  status via its own local type; Core consumers fail closed on unknown statuses
  (`llm.ts` maps any non-prepared status to `denied`).
- `open`/`openForSession`/`watchSession`: the layer captures `Database.Service`
  once at construction (as it already did `AppProcess.Service`); `open` keeps
  its runtime check that the observer source is the active Core database, but
  the `Interface` R stays `Scope` — no ripple into `prompt.ts`/`local.ts`.
- The verifier/root challenge validators and `validateGuardConsentProjection`
  are byte-identical to HEAD outside the returned-object literals (verified by
  diff against `git show HEAD:`).

## 13. 2026-10-23 — Full Access prepared-editor cancellation disposition (whole-phase continuation)

Verdict remains **PHASE_PARTIAL**. This continuation resolved the open
prepared-but-unspawned Full Access editor cancellation disposition in P1-owned
`kernel/src/effect_host.rs`, with TDD tests authored alongside. No Cargo job was
started: P2 still owns the single slot (no slot-free announcement; no
`cargo`/`rustc` process observed, but P2's report shows `check --tests` in
flight, so P1 held the slot rather than risk a duplicate heavy build).

### Disposition decision (from the live state machine, not a new policy)

`mark_external_editor_dispatching` is the atomic PREPARED→RUNNING fence whose
doc contract states "From this atomic point onward, a missing host response is
UNKNOWN and can never trigger a relaunch" — i.e. the fence commits **before**
any spawn. Therefore a durable row still in `PREPARED` proves no process was
ever created in any mode:

- **Prepared-but-unspawned cancellation is deterministic for Full Access.**
  `cancel_prepared_external_editor` no longer demands a cgroup identity. With a
  NULL identity it mints the deterministic
  `horizoncode.external-editor.pre-dispatch-cancel.v1` proof (new
  `editor_pre_dispatch_cancel_digest`, domain-separated from
  supervised-tree quiescence so it can never be mistaken for containment
  evidence), settles CANCELLED with a receipt, and settles the challenge
  SETTLED — all in the existing single transaction with the existing one-use
  `status='PREPARED'` guards. This closes the previously UNKNOWN-only path.
- **Post-dispatch Full Access stays UNKNOWN/no-replay** (unchanged): the
  RUNNING transition crosses the fence, so restart cannot prove absence and
  never fabricates a receipt.
- **Confined cancel now revalidates before killing.** The old code killed
  whatever cgroup the stored identity blob named without checking it against
  the row; it now requires `effect_id`/`execution_id`/`process_kind` to match
  the durable row before any `kill_and_confirm_empty`, else fail closed
  UNKNOWN. (Defense in depth: the blob was validated at prepare, but the
  cancel path must not trust a swapped row.)

### Supporting defects found and fixed in the same file

- `prepare_external_editor_execution` could not persist a Full Access run at
  all: it unconditionally decoded a cgroup identity, while
  `terminal_process::prepare_full_access` allocates none — making the
  previously added `None + DangerFullAccess` settle branch unreachable. The
  identity parameter is now `Option<&Value>` with an XOR gate: Full Access
  (`danger-full-access` + `never`) must pass `None` and can never claim a
  containment identity (`Some` → Denied); confined profiles must pass `Some`
  (`None` → Denied). The binding digest now binds
  `horizoncode.external-editor.no-cgroup.v1` when identity-less, the non-Linux
  platform stop applies to both arms, and the NULL identity persists through
  the existing nullable column.
- `reconcile_external_editor_processes` skipped NULL-identity rows entirely,
  leaving Full Access rows stuck forever after a restart. It now fences every
  non-terminal identity-less editor row to **receiptless UNKNOWN** (run +
  challenge) per the Full Access guarantee record — no relaunch, no fabricated
  receipt, no UNKNOWN→settled conversion without fresh authenticated evidence.

### Tests added (TDD; `#[cfg(test)] mod external_editor_disposition_tests`)

- `prepared_full_access_editor_cancel_is_deterministic_and_state_bound` —
  deterministic pre-dispatch proof persisted, receipt recomputed and
  cross-checked by `read_external_editor_outcome`, second cancel →
  `effect_conflict`, and `mark_external_editor_dispatching` after cancel →
  `effect_conflict` (the fence can never open after cancellation).
- `prepared_editor_cancel_rejects_identity_not_bound_to_its_row` — a stored
  identity naming another effect fails closed `effect_outcome_unknown`; run and
  challenge stay PREPARED with no manufactured receipt.
- `restart_reconcile_fences_full_access_editor_runs_to_receiptless_unknown` —
  both PREPARED and RUNNING identity-less rows become UNKNOWN with NULL
  quiescence and NULL receipt after restart reconciliation.
- `editor_prepare_rejects_contradictory_identity_projections` — confined +
  no identity → Denied; Full Access + identity → Denied; Full Access + no
  identity passes the projection gate and proceeds to the next missing durable
  authority (`approval_not_found`).

  authenticated request selector; rows must be ordered by `callIndex`, have
  bounded call IDs, and carry the expected terminal receipt fields. UNKNOWN
  rows require null receipt provenance and a null aggregate digest; known rows
  require typed receipt provenance. Thus the hashed values are exactly
  `[waveIndex, callIndex, callId, status, receiptKind, receiptRef,
  receiptDigest]` in row order.
- This matches the canonical v2 DTO/domain in `arch_V1/12-DOMAIN-SCHEMAS.md`
  §12.21, `arch_V1/contracts-v2/codemode-worker-message-v1.schema.json`,
  `kernel/src/codemode_protocol.rs`, and
  `packages/opencode/src/tool/code-mode-protocol.ts`. `callID` is the Core DTO
  spelling of the same call-ID value; the canonical digest is positional. The
  explicit NUL terminator and canonical MessagePack encoding also match. No
  P1-owned code correction is needed; no digest test was run in this
  continuation.

### 14.3 Failures repaired in this wave (root cause → fix → evidence)

**R1 — guard capability classification (the routed `language.server.start`
failure, `guard.rs`).** `LANGUAGE_SERVER_START_ID` and friends sit in **both**
`PROTOCOL_IO_CAPABILITY_IDS` and `is_broker_process_action`, and `authorize`
subtracted `&& !protocol_io_action` from `broker_action`, so process-control IDs
collapsed to pure protocol-IO: the §30.14 `model_process_control_denied` check
was unreachable (a model batch was refused with `unexpected_executable_binding`
instead) and a user-originated supervised start was denied outright.
Fix: broker semantics now win for the overlap (executable binding required and
validated, broker policy outranks protocol-IO policy below editor/code-mode),
and the §30.14 denial is gated on the capability ID + SessionBatch origin.
New regression tests `model_process_control_denial_precedes_the_protocol_io_executable_overlap`
and `managed_run_origin_labels_its_scope_session_but_never_carries_a_turn_context`.
Evidence: `cargo test --lib guard::` → 21/21 (was 19 passed/1 failed + 1 new
red before repair). A revert-and-rerun bisect proved the companion
`invalid_principal_or_origin` defect was independent of these edits (see R2).

**R2 — generic ManagedRun origin binding (`guard.rs`, born-red).** The generic
`ManagedRun` arm demanded `session_id` **empty** (`!session.is_empty() ||
!turn.is_empty()`), which rejected the Session-scoped managed verifier fixture
(session labelled) while the sibling provider arm requires the session —
verified byte-identical at HEAD via `git show` (committed red; the user held all
test runs). Fix: only a batch **turn context** disqualifies a ManagedRun; the
origin session label is scope-dependent (Session scope labels it, ProjectRead
scope leaves it empty). New contract test asserts both legitimate shapes plus
turn-context denial. Evidence: `builtin_verifier_challenge_...` (effect_host)
and `managed_run_approval_set_...` advanced from `effect_denied` to their next
stage.

**R3 — `existing_broker_process_set` incremental prepare (`effect_host.rs`).**
The `count != authorizations.len()` pre-check rejected a verifier batch built
member-by-member (one single-member prepare per call: count=1 from a sibling
member conflicted). Fix: per-member idempotent replay — each requested member
either replays (validated pins + AWAITING status) or is prepared fresh; only a
**torn atomic multi-member call** (some replay, some missing) still returns
`Conflict`. Evidence: `managed_run_approval_set_is_atomic_and_only_accepts_exact_all_allow`
progressed from `effect_conflict` to its resolve stage.

**R4 — Full Access direct-mode terminal (`effect_host.rs`).**
`classify_direct_mode_batch` counted Terminal as boundary-crossing even in
explicit Full Access, so Full Access + `Never` returned Deny. Fix: a narrow
`Terminal if full_access => {}` arm — the mode adds no local shell prompt;
ordinary Guard still governs `terminal.run` (Candidate ≠ allow). Evidence:
`explicit_full_access_skips_local_shell_prompt_but_keeps_search_consent_boundary`
green.

**R5 — `stdio_executions` fixtures missing `execution_principal` (born-red).**
Three fixtures inserted rows without `execution_principal` (schema default `''`)
while `mark_execution_unknown`/`execution_status`/`reconcile_stdio_execution_for_capability`
pin `execution_principal`/`row.27` against the binding → `approval_not_found`
/ `effect_denied`. Fixed all three fixtures (ambiguous dispatch, verifier
restart, stdio restart recovery). Evidence: those 3 tests green (module went
11→15 passing).

**R6 — missing verifier preflight binding (born-red contract drift).**
`managed_run_approval_set...` resolved all-allow without the durable
`managed_verifier_preflight` row the HANDOFF contract ("assessed launch profile
bound before approval") requires. Fixture now calls
`bind_managed_verifier_preflight` with an `assess_launch` assessment for every
challenge before resolve. Evidence: resolve step advanced past
`effect_denied`.

**R7 — two committed-red assertions (`effect_host.rs`).**
(a) `full_access_marker_is_exact_and_distinct_from_confined_launches` asserted
`marker != marker.trim_end_matches('.')` against a record with no trailing dot
— provably impossible as written (byte-identical at HEAD). Rewritten to the
asserted intent (prefix- and suffix-edited variants differ from the exact
constant). (b) `route_attempt_transition_requires_settled_retryable_receipts`
used the statusless adapter while the production gate (and the test's own name,
"requires settled retryable **receipts**") demands receipt status proof; the
`true` expectation is now expressed at the receipt level with `Some(503)` plus a
new negative pin (no status → no cross-route switch). Evidence: `effect_host::`

**R8 — `resource_sampler.rs` test import.** `aggregate_tests` referenced
`CACHE_EVICTION_OWNER` without importing it (blocked the whole lib-test binary
at compile). One-line `use super::{...}` fix.

**R9 — `tool_batch_multi_call` routed pair (both green).**
(a) `broker_process_capability_is_typed_unavailable_with_zero_effects`: the
fixture's broker call used the pre-rename string `language.server.start`, which

`effects_host_process`: (i) Unix socket paths exceeded `SUN_LEN` (~108 B) with

now fronts the authenticated Core identity (`ServerAuth { subject:

registration now carries `workspaceID` (owner check requires a registered

(`server_auth:effect-host-location-owner` / `server_auth:tool-batch-location-owner`
— the in-process entry derives the typed ServerAuth from a `server_auth:`

**R11 — `terminal_process.rs` PTY spawn seam.** `prepare_confined` returns
`cgroup: None, cgroup_required: true` (identity lives only in the HostKernel
binding) while `spawn_prepared` refuses to spawn without a prepared cgroup —
so `prepare_confined → spawn_prepared` was uncallable (born-red in
`be4f48c4d`). Added `PreparedTerminalLaunch::attach_execution_cgroup` (confirms
the exact empty owned group, then installs it) for the dispatcher (P2) and
tests. Two PTY acceptance tests use it under the delegation probe (§14.5).

### 14.4 Cross-phase blockers (exact interface / owner / evidence)

**B1 — P2-owned `host_kernel.rs`: root direct-session prepare is unsatisfiable
(affects 25 integration tests).**
`prepare_tool_batch` parses the turn-context digest with
`crate::guard::is_sha256_digest` (`host_kernel.rs:19558` — `sha256:` prefix
required, strict) and then, for every root direct-session batch, calls
`direct_session_batch_index_begin` with the same value, which requires
`is_blake3_digest(turn_context_digest)` (`host_kernel.rs:35183` — `blake3:`
prefix, strict). No single value can satisfy both, so **every** root
direct-session `prepare_tool_batch` returns `invalid_effect_request`
("effect request is malformed") before EffectHost is reached.
Evidence: (i) both lines are byte-present at HEAD (`git show HEAD:…`); (ii) a
transient in-process probe inside `prepare_tool_batch_internal` completed the
full P1 path with `status=mode_approval_candidate` when called directly, while
the integration runs reached **no** EffectHost entry (no probe output) with the
kernel-side error — probes were removed after diagnosis; (iii) after the
fixture repairs above, all three affected files' prepare failures are
uniformly `invalid_effect_request`.
Suggested direction (P2 decides): validate the turn-context digest with
`crate::guard::is_sha256_digest` in `direct_session_batch_index_begin` —

## 16. 2026-10-24 — Guard-origin audit and acceptance preparation

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL.** This continuation performs source review and
non-Cargo Core denial testing only. `GUARDED_PTY_RUNTIME_READY=false` and
`GUARDED_TERMINAL_RUN_READY=false` remain unchanged. P2 still owns the only
Cargo slot; no Rust compile/test was started, and no Git/provider/network action
was taken.

### Guard origin closure — source audit

- Re-ran the process-origin inventory over `packages/core/src`,
  `packages/opencode/src`, and `packages/tui/src` using
  `rg --no-ignore --hidden` (the overlay's blanket ignore otherwise hides
  sources). All Core `AppProcess.Service` `run`, `runStream`, and `spawn`
  requests pass through the P1 structural allowlist in
  `packages/core/src/process.ts`; its direct-spawn allowlist is limited to
  manifest-pinned `hz-kernel` / `session_supervisor`, while run/stream are
  limited to the pinned host-executable digest probe. Raw Bun/Node PTY adapters
  in `packages/core/src/pty/{pty.bun,pty.node}.ts` throw before native spawn.
  `packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts` has the two expected control-child
  launch sites; callers through OpenCode snapshot/git/worktree/format and Core
  git/ripgrep services otherwise use the guarded `AppProcess` service.
- **Cross-owner closure defect reported, not patched:**
  `packages/core/src/ripgrep/binary.ts:36-49` obtains the raw
  `ChildProcessSpawner` and starts arbitrary `tar` / `powershell.exe` commands
  directly at `:39`, bypassing `AppProcess`'s Guarded control-launch allowlist.
  The path is reachable from the Core ripgrep binary bootstrap and can download
  then extract an archive (`:104-119`). This is an effect-origin closure gap,
  not evidence that ordinary ripgrep query dispatch escapes its P1 path. The
  module is outside P1's listed exclusive write scope; parent must assign an
  owner to either replace this with a permitted supervised path or
  make bootstrap unavailable before the raw spawn. Do not enable an exception
  for tar/PowerShell in `AppProcess`. No new test was run for this finding; the
  focused source proof is the direct `ChildProcessSpawner` acquisition and
  `spawner.spawn` call above. Required negative: attempt binary bootstrap with
  an instrumented raw spawner and prove zero child starts on the unsupported
  path, plus ordinary query behavior when a trusted binary already exists.
- Focused existing denial suite, current tree:
  `cd packages/core && TMPDIR="$PWD" bun test test/process-guard.test.ts`
  → **3 pass / 0 fail, 10 assertions**. It proves generic run/stream/spawn,
  basename spoofing and executable replacement are denied before child process
  launch, and raw PTY adapters refuse execution. It does not cover the
  `ripgrep/binary.ts` bypass, so origin closure is NOT VERIFIED.

### Cgroup identity/cleanup and B4 matrix preparation

- Source review confirms `CgroupIdentityV1` binds boot ID, delegated-root and
  child device/inode/owner, relative path, effect/lease/principal/Attempt,
  owner epoch/process generation/kind, profile and executable digests, resource
  profile, and `cgroupHierarchyHidden`. Legacy decode forces owner UID to zero
  and hidden=false. `remove_quiescent_if_empty` requires hidden hierarchy plus
  exact recomputed persisted quiescence, reopens the canonical cgroup2 mount and
  exact owner/device/inode/path, checks the child is empty under both namespace
  and directory locks, then removes by name. Any failed proof retains/fails
  closed. Source tests cover false-hidden, stale proof, missing identity pins,
  and legacy retain-only; these are CODE_PRESENT, not fresh Rust evidence.
- `SandboxPreparation::proves_cgroup_hierarchy_hidden` returns true only for
  bubblewrap + a confined profile whose scoped roots and platform default reads
  do not overlap `/sys/fs/cgroup`. The actual mount namespace / process-tree
  behavior is not established by this predicate or the synthetic cgroup unit
  fixtures. The recorded host lacks a user-owned delegated cgroup root, so B4
  cannot be executed here; do not infer a pass from the lack of access.
- B4 run matrix is prepared for the next authorized delegated Linux runner:

  | Case | Setup | Required evidence / fail-closed result |
  |---|---|---|
  | B4-1 confined bubblewrap | Delegated root; probe child mount namespace and `/sys/fs/cgroup` access | Child cannot read/write host cgroup controls; durable identity hidden=true only from accepted profile; spawn admitted only with exact cgroup placement |
  | B4-2 root overlap | Confined profile with a scoped root equal to/inside/ancestor of `/sys/fs/cgroup` | Preparation denied before durable dispatch; no process/cgroup authority escapes |
  | B4-3 Full Access / no backend | Full Access, non-bubblewrap, or unsupported backend | hidden=false; no janitor deletion authority; unavailable where confinement is required |
  | B4-4 lease/owner/inode substitution | Mutate each persisted root/child owner, device, inode, path, lease, principal, Attempt, epoch or generation pin | Revalidation rejects before kill/removal; original group retained/quarantined |
  | B4-5 stale/absent quiescence | Missing, stale, mismatched or non-empty proof | no unlink; state remains retained/UNKNOWN; no receipt/retry fabricated |
  | B4-6 descendant escape and restart | Spawn confined process with descendants; crash/restart supervisor and reconcile | `cgroup.kill`/observed empty and exact persisted proof precede lease release/removal; ambiguous or escaped tree remains UNKNOWN and is never replayed |
  | B4-7 race/name replacement | Race replacement against cleanup under the namespace lock | cleanup removes only exact pinned inode/name while lock held; otherwise retains |

  Acceptance evidence must include kernel/backend/version, profile and mount
  view, persisted identity/quiescence digests, before/after cgroup membership,
  process-tree state, receipt/UNKNOWN state and exact test revision. Current
  state for every row: **NOT RUN**.

### `admitted_batch` is the correct outcome for this fixture

- The fixture registers a trusted direct-session Location with a workspace ID,
  then submits two `files.write` calls targeting files inside that workspace;
  it never changes the direct-session mode. HostKernel therefore resolves the
  canonical default `workspace-write + on-request` mode at
  `kernel/src/host_kernel.rs:21683-21714`.
- Normative §14.2 defines `needs_approval` as the ASK/challenge outcome, while
  §14.2 and §14.3 plus DEC-V1-28 say on-request permits supported work inside
  the selected sandbox and asks at its boundary. Thus these in-workspace writes
  are quiet candidates, not approval challenges. They still pass Guard and
  carry exact one-use leases; admission is not dispatch.
- Source agrees: `effect_host.rs::classify_direct_mode_batch` treats in-workspace
  `Write`/`Patch` as a mode candidate (`:17030-17073`); HostKernel performs its
  live mode/workspace recheck and only then issues each call's lease, returning
  `admitted_batch` (`host_kernel.rs:20135-20191`). Core's `EffectAdmission`
  explicitly models that status and its per-call challenge+lease
  (`packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts:902-915`).
- The test's unconditional `needs_approval` and per-member `resolve_approval`
  assertions were stale. Updated only the P1-owned matrix test to require
  `admitted_batch`, the exact batch and two call identities, each lease's
  effect-ID match and BLAKE3 lease digest, no workspace writes before dispatch,
  and two durable challenge rows. The follow-up single in-workspace write now
  asserts `prepared` plus its lease digest, likewise with no execution. No
  assertion was weakened and no approval semantics were substituted; explicit
  ASK coverage elsewhere remains intact.

## 27. 2026-10-24 — live bootstrap source-path audit after boundary incident

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL.** This continuation inspected repository source and
tests only to determine whether the live no-dispatch harness can be rerun with
all executable, temporary, state, and socket paths repository-contained. It did
not invoke the harness, inspect the outside executable, or retry either failed
case. Both terminal readiness gates remain false. No Cargo, HostKernel edit,
provider/network, Git mutation, publication, process inspection, or cleanup was
performed. A read-only Git MCP diff request in this continuation returned
truncated output and reported that it had stored a managed output artifact
outside the repository. That artifact was not opened, inspected, or removed; no
further Git MCP diff request was made after learning this behavior.

### Exact source findings

- `packages/core/test/terminal-run-live.test.ts:33-41` selects the HostKernel
  from `HZ_KERNEL_BIN` first, then `kernel/target/debug/hz-kernel`, then
  `target-p2/debug/hz-kernel`; only the latter two are statically repository-
  relative. A future invocation would have to supply a repository-contained
  `HZ_KERNEL_BIN` or rely on those candidates, and `hostKernelRuntime` reads and
  hashes the selected file at lines 118-133.
- The same test's `withLiveHostKernel` at lines 163-217 uses `tmpdir()` as the
  root for its `mkdtempSync` fixture, workspace, database, install roots, and
  HostKernel state roots. Therefore `TMPDIR` must be explicitly repository-
  contained; the test does not itself assert that containment.
- `packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts:3882-3894` derives the HostKernel
  socket under the supplied state directory as `host-<16 nonce chars>.sock`;
  `validateSocketPath` at lines 3568-3570 rejects UTF-8 paths of 108 bytes or
  more. The previous package-nested TMPDIR therefore failed before IPC. A
  repository-root or short repository-local `target` TMPDIR is a candidate for
  this path constraint, but the full resolved path still requires a preflight
  length/containment proof before any run.
- **Blocking executable path:** `supervisor.ts:3888-3892` passes
  `realpathSync(globalThis.process.execPath)` as `--host-exe`; the preceding
  digest-probe callsite at lines 4471-4495 obtains the same value and asks the
  pinned kernel to digest it. `establishChannel` at lines 3785-3789 also resolves
  `process.execPath`. The live test provides no override for these values.
  Consequently, a repo-contained kernel binary and TMPDIR alone do not make the
  invocation repository-contained. The Bun runtime's actual executable path
  must itself resolve inside the repository; this cannot be proven from the
  source/test files and would require forbidden inspection of the outside
  runtime path. No replacement path may be injected or substituted because
  this would falsify the production host-executable identity contract.
- The selected system shell/profile may also resolve to a host executable; the
  guarded-denial cases must prove its path before any live command is allowed.
  The test currently calls `Shell.discoverProfiles()` at lines 147-160 and does
  not constrain or assert that executable path to the repository.

### Decision and remaining evidence

- **Do not rerun `terminal-run-live.test.ts` under the current Bun runtime.** A
  statically compliant invocation/artifact path has not been demonstrated: both
  the runtime executable (`process.execPath`) and discovered shell executable
  are not source-controlled path inputs, and repository-only source review
  cannot establish their resolved locations. No test-only identity override,
  raw process fallback, or alternate Guard path was added.
- Safe future live acceptance requires a repository-contained runtime executable
  and kernel artifact, a repository-contained TMPDIR/state/workspace/database,
  and static/preflight proof that every generated socket is below 108 bytes;
  only then may the existing no-dispatch tests be considered for a fresh run.
  If those executable constraints cannot be met without touching an outside
  path, leave live acceptance NOT VERIFIED and keep both readiness gates false.
- Existing evidence remains strictly file-local/unit-level: `process-guard`
  **4/4 (13 expectations)**, Bash+ripgrep **5/5 (26 expectations)**, and
  supervisor launch/frame-binding **16/16 (47 expectations)**. The earlier live
  attempt remains **0/2 before HostKernel IPC**, not kernel denial evidence.
  B4 is unavailable; P1-10 remains P2/P7-owned; P1-11 remains parent-owned.
P2 owns Cargo and its HostKernel files; no P2 work was touched and no phase
dependency is newly unblocked.

  `kernel/src/protocol.rs` + `kernel/src/host_kernel.rs`; P7's owned caller is
  `packages/opencode/src/session/tool-batch.ts`. At the original handoff, source
  search found neither seam implemented. Parent now reports P2 is implementing
  the RPC and P7 has confirmed its separate private typed contract; P1 owns
  neither seam and changed neither file. This coordination update is not source
  or integration evidence.
- P2 must authenticate the existing direct Session/Location/owner fence and
  re-resolve current workspace/read scope in Rust; independently validate the
  whole bounded request (1–8 calls, canonical registered read-only capability
  IDs, per-call bounded arguments/resources, exact current scope/revision),
  reject unknown/mutation/terminal/provider/connector-write/question calls and
  malformed/oversized/mixed requests before returning any read authority, and
   bind every admitted call to its exact identity/scope/result budget. Parent
   has settled the §14.3.1/§37.24 ambiguity: use one batched RPC returning
   independent per-call opaque **read-only lease digests**. This keeps the
   one-RPC coalescing requirement while ensuring each digest authorizes only its
   exact read call; it is not a shared authority or a write/process lease. The
   RPC must not dispatch tools inside HostKernel.
- **Source-backed projection boundary:** neither §14.3.1 nor the §37 read-RPC
  paragraph names a concrete read-RPC wire DTO, digest preimage, or result-field
  list, so those
  must not be represented as already architecturally frozen. The minimum
  per-call projection is the exact current-response `callID`, canonical
  `capabilityID`, normalized-arguments/input digest, resolved target/scope,
  workspace revision where applicable, and bounded-result budget, associated
  with authenticated `sessionID`/`locationID`/`ownerEpoch` and the current
  permission/policy scope. This follows §14.3.1's per-read scope/digest/bounded
  result requirement and §37's authenticated direct read admission. The current
  Core `EffectLease` DTO is effect-scoped (`effectID` + opaque
  `capabilityLeaseDigest`), while `DirectSessionPermissionModeV1` carries the
  session, owner epoch, location/workspace-root, policy, mode revision and
  digest; neither is itself a specified read-batch DTO. P7 confirms it uses a
  separate private typed read contract and does not widen or reuse terminal/
  write `EffectLease`. P2 owns the Rust RPC implementation; P7 owns stream-time
  classification and dispatch through the typed read path. Rust remains the
  authority, Core must not synthesize or widen lease scope, and every result/
  receipt must bind back to its exact call and authenticated scope.
- P7 must classify only schema-validated current-response reads, coalesce up to
  eight into one RPC, call the existing typed read-tool path (never invoke tool
  implementations directly), and leave mutations/unknown calls buffered until
  the complete response is closed and ordinary `prepare_tool_batch` succeeds.
  Already-settled reads remain valid if a later response member is invalid;
  invalid new members must start zero additional reads. Managed Attempts keep
  the fast path off by default.
- Shared acceptance required before marking P1-10 wired: reads demonstrably
  start before stream close; mutations wait; >1 read produces one RPC; malformed,
  unknown, mixed, >8, stale owner/scope/revision and read-lease substitution
  negatives dispatch zero reads; each settled read has bounded result/digest
  and exact owner/scope attribution; restart/cancel ambiguity does not replay.
  Then P1/P2/P7 run the §32.49 PERF-DIRECT-002/004 parity evidence.

## 34. 2026-10-24 — P6 handoff: `SessionV2.revert` fails closed (sdk-next TS2554) + integrated HTTP auth denial

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL (unchanged).** Both terminal readiness gates stay
`false`; no Rust command, Git write, provider/network/paid call, or worktree was
performed by this continuation. `Cargo` was **occupied by another task-owned job**
(`cargo test --manifest-path …` plus `rustc` children observed at start and during
this wave), so P1 deliberately ran no Cargo and did not re-run the retained-green
Rust suites.

### 34.1 Root cause adopted from P6 (verified first-hand, not assumed)

- `packages/sdk-next/src/opencode.ts:32` needed `web.handler(request)` as a
  **one-argument** call. `HttpRouter.toWebHandler` types `handler` as
  `(request, context?)` only when `HR = Exclude<Requires|GlobalRequires, A>` is
  `never`; a single unsatisfied required service forces
  `(request, context: Context<HR>)`, i.e. **TS2554**.
- P6's line map was re-read and confirmed:
  `packages/server/src/middleware/authorization.ts` provides
  `TrustedPrincipalContext` at **:55/:59/:66/:80** (ticketed/authenticated or
  trusted-loopback branches) and **not** at **:62/:69/:79** — exactly the
  unauthenticated non-loopback branches, where supplying `host_os` would be a
  Guard bypass and where `TrustedPrincipal` has no untrusted kind.
- The type-level requirement reached the router through
  `packages/core/src/session.ts:188-200` (`SessionV2.Interface.revert.stage/clear`
  declared `R = TrustedPrincipalContext`) consumed by
  `packages/server/src/handlers/session.ts:256-306`.

### 34.2 Test-first: RED proved the seam did not enforce a principal at all

New `packages/core/test/session-revert-principal.test.ts` (3 tests; runner
deliberately provides **no** `TrustedPrincipalContext` for the negatives — it
never fabricates `host_os`/`server_auth`, and the positive case supplies a genuine
`server_auth` principal).

RED before the fix (`TMPDIR="$PWD" bun test test/session-revert-principal.test.ts`
→ **1 pass / 2 fail**):

- `stage` without a principal **succeeded** — the program returned the staged state
  `{"messageID":"msg_revert_boundary","diff":"","restoreFiles":[]}` (so an
  unauthenticated caller mutated Session revert state);
- `clear` without a principal **succeeded** (the flip produced
  `Unknown error: undefined`);
- the `server_auth` control case already passed.

### 34.3 Fix (P6's recommendation adopted verbatim: fail closed with `Effect.serviceOption`)

`packages/core/src/session.ts` only:

- `revert.stage` / `revert.clear` now read
  `yield* Effect.serviceOption(TrustedPrincipalContext)` first and fail with the
  typed `SessionSupervisorError` —
  `{ stage: "authorization", reason: "Authenticated principal required to stage a Session revert" }`
  / `… clear a Session revert"` — matching the existing precedents at
  `session.ts:233/408/485` and `resume`.
- The interface declaration lost its `TrustedPrincipalContext` `R` on both methods;
  the error unions are unchanged (`SessionSupervisorError` was already declared),
  so the server handlers' `mapSupervisorError` → `ForbiddenError` (403) path needed
  no edit. No other file, no `authorization.ts` branch, and no principal provider
  was touched.
- `revert.commit` was deliberately **not** changed: its protocol endpoint
  (`packages/protocol/src/groups/session.ts`) declares only `SessionNotFoundError`,
  so a Core-only denial there would surface as an undeclared error. Recorded below
  as a cross-phase request.

### 34.5 Integrated HTTP proof (new `packages/server/test/session-revert-authorization.test.ts`)

Drives the **real** `createEmbeddedRoutes()` / `createRoutes("secret")` stacks
through `HttpRouter.toWebHandler` with one `web.handler(request)` argument (the
arity P9 could not typecheck before), seeded through the same `Layer.MemoMap` the
route layer consumes:

1. **Unauthenticated non-loopback `revert.stage`** → **403**
   `{"_tag":"ForbiddenError","message":"Session supervisor authorization failed: Authenticated principal required to stage a Session revert"}`,
   zero `*.revert.*` events, `SessionTable.revert` still `NULL`.
2. **Unauthenticated non-loopback `revert.clear`** with a pre-staged revert →
   **403** with the clear reason, the staged row **preserved**, zero new events.
3. **Password-required stack**: anonymous **401**, wrong Basic credential **401**,
   correct `server_auth` Basic credential → stage **200** with the staged state,
   clear **204**, events exactly `revert.staged.1` + `revert.cleared.1`, final
   `revert` `NULL` — i.e. existing authenticated behavior is unchanged.

The middleware's trusted-loopback branch was re-read as part of this: it requires a
real `remoteAddress` and rejects any `forwarded`/`x-real-ip`/`x-forwarded-*`
header, so these `toWebHandler` requests cannot be mistaken for loopback peers.

## 35. 2026-10-24 — P1-10 client seam (blockers 1–2) and fail-closed TrustedPrincipalContext

> Numbering note: this section was appended as a second `§34` by a concurrent P1
> continuation; the earlier `§34` (the revert fail-closed fix + integrated HTTP
> denial evidence) stays `§34`, so this continuation is `§35`. No content changed.

Parent routed two P7-found seam blockers into the P1-owned
`packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts`, plus the P6-routed fail-closed
principal fix. Focused tests only (A1 owns the TypeScript slot); no Cargo (P2
holds the slot), no Git, no provider/network, no P2/P7/P8/P9 source edits.

### 1. Blocker 1 — the allowlisted read was rejected at the client (RED→GREEN)

`HostKernelClientImpl.request` accepted a private `repo_read` context only for
`repo-intel.v1`, so `effects.v2/prepare_tool_batch_read` could never be sent
even though `requestHostKernel` already allowlisted it.

Source change (client only, every prior check preserved):

- added `const isDirectReadBatch = serviceID === "effects.v2" && operation ===
  "prepare_tool_batch_read"` and added it to the outer admission disjunction, so
  a read batch **without** a context now fails closed at the client instead of
  being written to the wire;
- inside the `repo_read` branch the condition is now: neither allowlisted
  operation → deny; repo-intel read → unchanged `repo-intel.read-context-v1`
  feature check; direct read batch → negotiated `effects.v2` **and**
  `validDirectReadBatchAuthenticatedRequest(...)` (exact service/operation,
  `schemaVersion: 1`, body Session/Location equal to the context, 1..8 calls);
  plus the unchanged `ses_`/`loc_` canonical-shape regexes. Any other
  `effects.v2` operation under `repo_read`, any feature-less negotiation and any
  body/context scope mismatch stay denied;
- `requestHostKernel`'s own gate gained the same `isDirectReadBatch` disjunct
  (defense in depth: a missing context fails closed with the typed
  `authenticated read context is unavailable` before the client is reached).
  No context kind, feature string or operation set was widened.

**RED (exact):** with only the mechanical test seam exported and no clause, the
new client test reported `4 pass / 6 fail / 20 expectations` and

```
error: Session supervisor protocol failed: authenticated read context is unavailable or invalid
  stage: "protocol",
 reason: "authenticated read context is unavailable or invalid",
      at request (.../packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts:4176:15)
      at .../test/session-supervisor-read-batch-client.test.ts:259:30
```

**GREEN:**

```sh
cd packages/core && TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-independent" bun test \
  test/session-supervisor-read-batch-client.test.ts
# 10 pass / 0 fail / 47 expectations
```

### 2. Blocker 2 — caller-supplied envelope `requestID` (RED→GREEN)

The envelope ID was minted at the client and discarded when `#receive`
resolved with `response.body` only, so Core could not bind projection rows to
an identity Rust had already written into every row.

- `request()` gained an optional 8th `options.requestID`, validated strictly
  **before any frame is written**: `^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,256}$` **and** the
  operation must be the P1-10 read batch, otherwise
  `fail("protocol", "RPC request id is invalid")`. Absent →
  `req_<uuid32>` exactly as before, so every existing caller is byte-identical;
- `Interface.requestHostKernel` / `requestHostKernel` options gained
  `readonly requestID?: string`, forwarded as `{ requestID }` only when
  defined. The wire DTO is untouched (the envelope already carried
  `requestId`); only the P1-10 path may set it — no other caller passes it yet;
- test seam: exported structural `HostKernelTransport` (the `MessageChannel`
  surface actually used) and `export class HostKernelClientImpl`. Production
  still constructs the client only in `establishHostChannel` over the
  negotiated host socket.

**RED:** before the parameter existed the same file ran `4 pass / 6 fail`, with
the echo case failing on `expect(sent[0].requestId).toBe("rpc_read_1")` (client
had minted its own) and the malformed/foreign-ID cases failing on
`Expected substring: "RPC request id is invalid"`.

**GREEN** (same command as above, `10 pass / 0 fail / 47 expectations`):
supplied ID echoed in the inspected envelope and round-tripped through the
response; 256-character boundary ID accepted; absent ID preserves the
generated `req_[0-9a-f]{32}` and still resolves; empty, spaced, slashed,
control-byte and 260/516-character IDs rejected before send; a caller ID on any
other operation rejected before send.

```sh
  test/session-supervisor-launch.test.ts
# 53 pass / 0 fail / 541 expectations
```

(P7-owned file, not edited here): wiring `requestID: input.requestID` into its

### 4. Fail-closed `TrustedPrincipalContext` (P6 root-cause map)

`packages/core/src/session.ts` now reads the principal with
`Effect.serviceOption(TrustedPrincipalContext)` at **every** principal-dependent
seam — `create` :232, `prompt` :407, `resume` :484, `revert.stage` :502,
`revert.clear` :523 — and the `Interface` type no longer carries a type-level

yields a typed `SessionSupervisorError { stage: "authorization" }` **before any
read or mutation**, and Core never fabricates `host_os` (or any principal) on an
unauthenticated branch.

## 36. 2026-10-25 — cross-phase P2 request: managed CodeMode wave reader / UNKNOWN fence

**Request (verbatim from P2's blocker, relayed by the parent):** *"P1-owned
`effect_host.rs` does not expose a public managed CodeMode wave outcome reader or
UNKNOWN fence. HostKernel cannot read/fence managed wave receipts without it;
requesting `pub(crate)` exposure (no logic change)."* Symbols:
`read_code_mode_wave_outcome`, `fence_code_mode_unknown`; required callable from
`host_kernel.rs`.

### 1. Finding: the exposure already exists, visibility-only

Both symbols were **already `pub(crate)` in the working tree at session start**
(uncommitted earlier P1 work), and `git diff -U4 HEAD -- kernel/src/effect_host.rs`
shows exactly two changed lines for them, with every surrounding line unchanged:

```
-    fn read_code_mode_wave_outcome(&self,request_id:&str,wave_index:u8,batch_id:&str)->Result<CodeModeWaveOutcomeV1,TypedErrorV1>{
+    pub(crate) fn read_code_mode_wave_outcome(&self,request_id:&str,wave_index:u8,batch_id:&str)->Result<CodeModeWaveOutcomeV1,TypedErrorV1>{
-    fn fence_code_mode_unknown(&mut self, request_id: &str, batch_id: &str) -> Result<(), TypedErrorV1> {
+    pub(crate) fn fence_code_mode_unknown(&mut self, request_id: &str, batch_id: &str) -> Result<(), TypedErrorV1> {
```

Current locations: `kernel/src/effect_host.rs:11036` and `:11344`. `host_kernel.rs`
is in the same crate, so `pub(crate)` is sufficient and no `pub` widening, no
signature, return-shape, validation, digest, receipt-derivation or fencing change
is needed or was made. Call sites already present in P2-owned
`kernel/src/host_kernel.rs`: `codemode_wave_receipts` → `:4436`,
`codemode_cancel_wave` → `:4457` (read) and `:4462` (fence). **No production line
was edited by this continuation.**

### 2. Test-first: no existing test covered the three preconditions

Checked first, not duplicated. What exists is digest/protocol-level only:
`effect_host::codemode_wave_receipt_tests::{wave_receipt_digest_is_ordered_and_binds_batch_and_member_receipts,
direct_session_code_mode_has_no_attempt_and_refreshes_workspace_grant_per_wave,
terminal_decision_receipt_binds_decision_evidence_and_has_separate_ref_domain}`
and `codemode_protocol::{wave_results_must_correlate_in_exact_call_order,
settled_tool_result_requires_matching_digest_and_utf8_byte_count,
known_rejected_result_is_terminal_and_allows_one_failed_completion,
cancelled_wave_allows_only_one_helper_completion_and_unknown_allows_none,
one_wave_argument_bytes_cannot_exceed_toolbatch_limit}`. **None** reads
`code_mode_waves` through `read_code_mode_wave_outcome` or applies
`fence_code_mode_unknown`, so new tests were added in a new
`#[cfg(test)] mod codemode_wave_reader_fence_tests` (`effect_host.rs:16720+`),
test-only — no production behavior touched:

| Test | Precondition asserted |
|---|---|
| `committed_wave_outcome_is_the_only_terminal_read` | **Non-vacuity control**: a fully committed wave (wave `receipt_digest` = `outcome_digest` = `codemode_wave_receipt_digest` over the exact retained tuples, retained call row, per-call `effect_receipt` row) still resolves **Ok `settled`** with the matching settlement digest — so the refusals below are not an always-error. |
| `wave_without_a_committed_outcome_stays_unknown` | Waves `RESERVED`/`ADMITTED`/`BOUND`/`UNKNOWN` with (a) no receipt, (b) receipt but no outcome, (c) receipt ≠ outcome → each `effect_outcome_unknown`; a foreign request id → `approval_not_found` (absent, never terminal). |
| `ambiguous_receipt_rows_never_resolve_to_a_terminal_value` | A committed aggregate over durable rows that cannot substantiate it — no result rows at all, a non-BLAKE3 receipt digest, a NULL `receipt_kind` → each `effect_outcome_unknown`, never a terminal value. |
| `unknown_fence_never_applies_to_a_foreign_or_already_fenced_wave` | Foreign request → wave **and** run unchanged; foreign batch for a real request → that wave unchanged (the run-level fence is keyed on request authority only — existing semantics, documented and **not** changed); exact request+batch → wave `UNKNOWN`; a second fence is a no-op (never a rewrite/reset); an already-`SETTLED` wave and its receipt stay untouched; the other request's wave/run never move. |

### 3. Exact commands and results

Every command from `kernel/` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-p2`,
`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1`, and a `pgrep -x cargo` slot check immediately before it.

## 37. 2026-10-25 — `session.revert.commit` principal gate (RED→GREEN) + HTTP 401 boundary

> Numbering note: the parent asked for "a new §36", but a concurrent P1 continuation
> had already appended its `effect_host.rs` exposure request as **§36** (line 2719)
> before this continuation reached the report. To honour "don't renumber sections
> again" *and* avoid a duplicate section number, this continuation is **§37**. No
> existing section was renumbered, moved or edited.

**Scope honored:** `packages/core/src/session.ts` + the two P1-owned revert test files,
plus the one compiler-proven handler dependency disclosed in §37.3. No Rust, no Cargo,
no `kernel/src/effect_host.rs` (that is §36's session), no Git, no provider/network, no
worktree, no `host_os`/`server_auth` fabricated anywhere — every negative runs with
**no** `TrustedPrincipalContext` at all. Both terminal readiness flags stay `false`.

### 37.1 Ownership confirmed first-hand (not assumed)

- `packages/core/src/session.ts:545` is `V2Session.revert.commit`; it is the *same file,
  same seam, same mechanism* as the `revert.stage`/`revert.clear` fix in §34/§35.
- **Exhaustive caller check** (`grep -rn "revert\.commit" packages --include=*.ts`,
  excluding `node_modules`, `/dist/`, generated): the **only** Core caller is
  `packages/server/src/handlers/session.ts:307`. Everything else is an event-type string
  (`session.next.revert.committed` in `schema/session-event.ts:502`,
  `app/context/server-session.ts:981`, `core/session/message-updater.ts:415`), the
  generated SDK/client wrappers, or the HTTP route row in
  `packages/opencode/test/server/httpapi-exercise/index.ts:1028`. There is therefore
  **no** second Core path that can reach `revert.commit` without passing through the
  gate.
- `packages/app/src/utils/server-compat.ts:298` (`commit:
  input.current.session.revert.commit`) is the *generated client* function on
  `ServerApi`, i.e. it goes over HTTP through the same gated handler — not a bypass.
- **P7's `options.requestID` — confirmed now present in source.** §35 recorded this as
  outstanding; the caller has since landed it at
  `packages/core/src/session/direct-read-batch.ts:569-575` —
  `input.host.requestHostKernel("effects.v2", "prepare_tool_batch_read", request, {
  trustedPrincipal, authenticatedContext, requestID: input.requestID })`. The suite
  covering it is green below (**27/0/447**). Source presence verified by P1; P7 still
  owns declaring it done and owns the runner-wiring decision.

### 37.2 RED — with no principal, `commit` succeeded and destroyed durable state

New case in `packages/core/test/session-revert-principal.test.ts` (the runner supplies
**no** `TrustedPrincipalContext`), plus a post-boundary message seeded at `seq: 2` so the
deletion is observable. `SessionRevert.commit` publishes `revert.committed`, whose
projector (`packages/core/src/session/projector.ts:445-483`) deletes every message with
`seq > boundary.seq`, deletes the session's inputs and nulls `SessionTable.revert`.

Durable-state assertions are deliberately placed **before** the typed-denial assertion,
so a pre-fix run prints the mutation instead of stopping at the missing error:

```
expect(outcome.value.events).toEqual([])
error: expect(received).toEqual(expected)

- []
+ [
+   "session.next.revert.committed.1",
+ ]

 4 pass
 1 fail
 15 expect() calls
Ran 5 tests across 1 file. [52.10s]
```

So an unauthenticated caller could (a) write `session.next.revert.committed.1`, (b)
null the staged revert and (c) delete the post-boundary message. `Effect.flip` (the
style used for stage/clear) is deliberately **not** used in this case: `commit`
succeeds with `void`, so a flip converts the pre-fix success into an opaque
`Unknown error: undefined` failure that aborts the program before the durable state can
be read (that exact signature is what §34.2 recorded for `clear`). The case captures the
typed failure with `Effect.map(() => undefined) → Effect.catch`, so both outcomes stay
observable while defects still propagate.

### 37.3 GREEN — same mechanism as stage/clear, and the one compiler-proven dependency

`packages/core/src/session.ts` only:

- `:545-561` — `Effect.serviceOption(TrustedPrincipalContext)` **first**, then typed
  `SessionSupervisorError { stage: "authorization", reason: "Authenticated principal
  required to commit a Session revert" }`, then `result.get`, then the mutation.
  Denial is strictly before any read or write.
- `:197-199` — `Interface.revert.commit` error union widened to
  `Effect.Effect<void, NotFoundError | SessionSupervisorError>`.

**`R` is unchanged, not removed — there was nothing to remove.** Unlike stage/clear,
`commit` never declared `R = TrustedPrincipalContext` (its signature was
`Effect.Effect<void, NotFoundError>`, i.e. `R = never`), so the required change is an
*error-union* widening rather than an `R` removal. The guard is still fail-closed rather
than a type-level `R`, exactly as for stage/clear.

**The parent's "no protocol or handler change" premise held for the protocol and failed
for the handler. Quoting the compiler, as instructed.** With only the Core change in
place:

```
$ cd packages/server && TMPDIR="$PWD" ../../node_modules/.bin/tsgo --noEmit
src/handlers/session.ts(305,9): error TS2345: Argument of type '(ctx: {...}) => Effect<...>' is not assignable to parameter of type 'HandlerWithName<HttpApiEndpoint<"session.context", ...> | ... 5 more ... | HttpApi...>'.
  Type 'Effect<void, SessionNotFoundError | SessionSupervisorError, never>' is not assignable to type 'Effect<void | HttpServerResponse, InvalidRequestError | ServiceUnavailableError | SessionNotFoundError | UnauthorizedError, never>'.
    Type 'SessionNotFoundError | SessionSupervisorError' is not assignable to type 'InvalidRequestError | ServiceUnavailableError | SessionNotFoundError | UnauthorizedError'.
      Type 'SessionSupervisorError' is not assignable to type 'InvalidRequestError | ServiceUnavailableError | SessionNotFoundError | UnauthorizedError'.
        Type 'SessionSupervisorError' is not assignable to type 'UnauthorizedError'.
          Types of property '_tag' are incompatible.
            Type '"SessionSupervisorError"' is not assignable to type '"UnauthorizedError"'.
SERVER_EXIT=2
```

That is the genuine type-system blocker: `SessionSupervisorError` is in the Core error
channel the handler must consume, and it is not assignable to the endpoint's effective
error set, so **some** handler-side mapping is unavoidable. The compiler message also
shows exactly which errors *are* allowed — `InvalidRequestError | ServiceUnavailableError
| SessionNotFoundError | UnauthorizedError` (endpoint-declared `SessionNotFoundError`
plus the API-level `Authorization`/`SchemaErrorMiddleware` errors). Notably
`ForbiddenError` is **not** among them.

Minimal resolution applied (`packages/server/src/handlers/session.ts:304-324`):
`Effect.catchTag` → `Effect.mapError`, mapping `Session.NotFoundError` exactly as before
and the Core principal denial to `UnauthorizedError` (401) with the verbatim message.
**`packages/protocol/src/groups/session.ts` was NOT touched** — `session.revert.commit`
still declares `error: SessionNotFoundError` only, so the public endpoint contract,
its OpenAPI error list and the generated clients are unchanged. `UnauthorizedError`
(401 = "no authenticated principal") is honest for this case and is the same status the
`Authorization` middleware itself returns when credentials are absent.

*Deviation disclosed for parent audit:* this is a **handler** edit outside P1's nominal
`session.ts`+tests write scope, made because (a) the compiler proves a Core-only gate
cannot reach the HTTP surface otherwise, (b) the parent's own required HTTP "commit row"
cannot pass without it, and (c) "keep `tsgo` at 0" is unconditional. It adds **no** new
declared response to the contract. If the parent prefers 403 consistency with stage/clear,
the alternative is a one-line protocol edit (`error: [SessionNotFoundError, ForbiddenError]`
at `packages/protocol/src/groups/session.ts:293`) plus `mapSupervisorError` in the handler;
that **is** a public-contract change and is deliberately **not** done here.

Known inconsistency to record: `revert.stage`/`revert.clear` answer **403** for the same
missing-principal condition (§34) while `revert.commit` now answers **401**. Both deny;
the difference is forced by the endpoint's already-declared error set. Unifying them
needs the protocol decision above.

Existing rows are untouched and still pass: stage-denied 403 + no mutation, clear-denied
403 + staged row preserved, and authenticated stage 200 / clear 204 with exactly

> Numbering note: appended as **§38**; §36 (concurrent `effect_host.rs` session) and §37 (the
> `revert.commit` gate) already exist. No existing section was renumbered, moved or edited.

**Parent decision adopted:** the §37 handler deviation is ratified, and the status inconsistency is
resolved by making the contract change — all three `session.revert.*` operations are one API family
and must deny identically. 401 stays reserved for bad credentials.

### 38.1 Derivation check performed first (as instructed), before touching the contract

| `packages/sdk/js/src/v2/gen/*` | **Byte-generated.** `script/build.ts:14` runs `bun dev generate > openapi.json` in `packages/opencode`, whose `src/server/routes/instance/httpapi/api.ts:34` imports `makeApi` from `@opencode-ai/protocol/api`, then `@hey-api/openapi-ts` with `output.clean: true` (wholesale rewrite). Declared errors **do** flow in: `V2SessionRevertStageErrors` already carries `403: ForbiddenError`. | **YES → moves.** `V2SessionRevertCommitErrors` (`types.gen.ts:13913`) would gain `403: ForbiddenError`. |
| `packages/app/src/utils/server-compat.ts` | **Hand-written** (`src/`, not generated). Uses `ServerApi = OpenCodeClient` and only `Parameters<Fn>[0]` (input params) plus a bare value reference `commit: input.current.session.revert.commit`. | **NO → unaffected.** Adding an error-union member changes no input parameter type. Nothing to regenerate. |
| `packages/client/src/generated/*` (not on your list; found by the check) | **Byte-generated.** `script/build.ts` → `@opencode-ai/httpapi-codegen`; `src/index.ts:520` `const declaredStatuses = [...new Set(endpoint.errors.map((error) => error.status))]`, status from the Schema `httpApiStatus` annotation (`errors.ts:101` → 403). | **YES → moves.** `client.ts:433` `declaredStatuses: [404, 400, 401]` would become `[404, 403, 400, 401]`. `generated/types.ts` and `generated-effect/client.ts` are **unaffected** (types.ts does not emit `ForbiddenError` even for stage/clear; the effect client only reads `Parameters<…>[0]`). |

**Verdict: regeneration is genuinely required.** Per your bound it was **not run** — §38.5 reports the
exact files/churn for your review instead.

### 38.2 RED — the family did not deny identically

New table in `packages/server/test/session-revert-authorization.test.ts`, one shared `revertOps`
table driving both directions, seeded with a direct-SQL staged revert (writes no event, so any event
is one the request itself produced) plus the post-boundary message:

```
error: expect(received).toEqual(expected)

@@ -2,3 +2,3 @@
    "op": "commit",
-   "status": 403,
+   "status": 401,

(fail) revert HTTP authorization boundary > stage, clear and commit all deny with 403 when no trusted principal is present
 6 pass  1 fail  41 expect() calls
```

Exactly the predicted failure: `stage` and `clear` already returned **403**, `commit` returned **401**,
and the bad-Basic table already returned **401** for all three.

### 38.3 GREEN — contract + handler

- `packages/protocol/src/groups/session.ts:293` → `error: [SessionNotFoundError, ForbiddenError]`
  (additive; `ForbiddenError` was already imported at `:12` and already declared by stage/clear).
- `packages/server/src/handlers/session.ts:304-331` → the denial now maps to
  `new ForbiddenError({ message: error.message })`; the now-unused `UnauthorizedError` import was
  removed.

**`mapSupervisorError` could not be called verbatim — compiler quoted, as with §37.3.** With the
authorized array in place and `return mapSupervisorError(error, ctx.params.sessionID)`:

```
src/handlers/session.ts(305,9): error TS2345: ...
  Type 'Effect<void, ConflictError | ForbiddenError | ServiceUnavailableError | SessionNotFoundError, never>' is not assignable to
  type 'Effect<void | HttpServerResponse, ForbiddenError | InvalidRequestError | ServiceUnavailableError | SessionNotFoundError | UnauthorizedError, never>'.
    Type 'ConflictError' is not assignable to type 'ForbiddenError | InvalidRequestError | ServiceUnavailableError | SessionNotFoundError | UnauthorizedError'.
      Types of property '_tag' are incompatible.
        Type '"ConflictError"' is not assignable to type '"UnauthorizedError"'.
SERVER_EXIT=2
```

`mapSupervisorError`'s declared return is `ForbiddenError | ConflictError | ServiceUnavailableError`.
Only the `ConflictError` (409, `stage: "ownership"`) arm fails: it is neither producible by
`revert.commit`'s gate nor declared on this endpoint. Your two instructions were therefore mutually
exclusive for this endpoint, and I resolved it **in favour of your exact contract array** —
declaring 409/503 would have *exceeded* the additive `error: [SessionNotFoundError, ForbiddenError]`
bound you authorized and would have multiplied the generated churn you asked me to keep minimal.

What is inlined is `mapSupervisorError`'s `stage === "authorization"` arm **byte-for-byte**: its own
`:26` is `return new ForbiddenError({ message: error.message })`. So the runtime result, status and
body are identical to stage/clear's path for this condition; only the helper *call* is local, with the
reason documented at the call site. If you prefer the helper literally, the alternative is widening
to `error: [SessionNotFoundError, ConflictError, ForbiddenError, ServiceUnavailableError]` (mirroring
`clear`) — a larger contract + churn delta, **not** done.

### 39.4 Supervisor startup diagnostics — RED → GREEN (test-first, fake child)

**Defect:** `packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts:3663` rejected with only
`supervisor exited before binding its socket (<path>)`, and both spawn sites passed
`stderr: "ignore"`, so the child's own output was discarded. The E2E burned four attempts
(including `--print-logs --log-level DEBUG`) on a message that could never explain itself.

**RED** (new cases in the existing, Git-visible `packages/core/test/session-supervisor-launch.test.ts`,
fake child built from `isRunning`/`exitCode`/`stderr`, no principal or kernel involved):

```
(fail) ... reports exit status and stderr when the child exits before binding its socket
  expect(message).toContain("exit code 7")            ← expected text absent
(fail) ... reports exit status and stderr when the child exits before securing its socket
  expect(message).toContain("exit code 2")
 16 pass  2 fail  51 expect() calls
```

**GREEN** — `packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts` only:

- both spawn sites (`:3918` host-kernel, `:4684` session_supervisor) → `stderr: "pipe"`;
- new bounded capture `captureChildStderr` drains the pipe into an **8 KiB** buffer as soon as the
  child spawns (a piped-but-unread stderr fills the OS pipe and would block the child — the drain is
  not optional once `"pipe"` is set), marked `… (stderr truncated at 8192 bytes)` when full;
- `describeChildExit` resolves `child.exitCode` and the tail, both raced against a **500 ms** bound
  with `clearTimeout`, so a diagnostic can never hang the failure path;
- both child-death branches now append `; exit code N; stderr: <tail>` (or
  `; exit status unavailable`).

```
$ cd packages/core && TMPDIR="$PWD" bun test test/session-supervisor-launch.test.ts
 18 pass  0 fail  53 expect() calls
```

No lease check, permission check, socket-ownership check or readiness flag was touched; the
enrichment is purely additive message content. `tsgo --noEmit` for `packages/core` → **exit 0**.

### 39.5 App-path vs standalone — diagnosis and the hand-off

The kernel-side precondition is already established (P6 §5.1): `InsecureDirectory` wants dirs
`0700`, `UnsafeFile` wants `supervisor.lock`/`supervisor.epoch` `0600`, and the same binary binds
standalone once those hold. The app path creates its state directory with
`ensurePrivateDirectory` at **`packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts:2430-2460`**, which does
enforce owner-only (`mkdirSync(path, { mode: 0o700 })`, `realpath === path`, no symlink, uid match,
parent `(mode & 0o022) === 0`). So the directory the app *creates* is compliant; what is not yet
observable is **which** of the two child-death branches fired and what the child printed — exactly
what §39.4 now surfaces. The next E2E attempt should be read against the enriched message before any
further root-cause claim; I am not asserting a cause I could not observe.

**Two failures are outside `supervisor.ts` — reported, not edited:**

1. **`run`'s opaque `Session not found`** — `packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/run/runtime.ts:757-759`,
   inside `resolveSession`: `if (!next?.id) { throw new Error("Session not found") }`. The cause is
   discarded, so an underlying session/bring-up failure is reported as a plain not-found with exit 1
   and no chain. Needed change (owner **P9**, CLI/runtime boot): keep the failure but carry the
   cause (`throw new Error("Session not found", { cause })`) or surface the upstream error, so a
   bring-up defect is not misreported as a missing session. Supporting site:
   `packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/run.ts:461-466` swallows `sdk.session.get` failures with
   `.catch(() => undefined)` before `UI.error("Session not found"); process.exit(1)`.
   Both files are tracked and unmodified; **not edited by P1.**
2. **`serve`'s bring-up** is the same §39.4 path — already P1's, now diagnosable.

3. **DEFERRED (parent call, 2026-10-25): the SDK `V2SessionRevertCommitErrors` 403 stays
   unreconciled.** The contract/handler ratification in §38 (403 family-wide) is **not** reflected in
   `packages/sdk/js/src/v2/gen/types.gen.ts`, because the regeneration that would have produced it
   was reverted (§39.8) and its build is broken. **Named defect:** `packages/sdk/js/script/build.ts:174`
   — `patchGoalReviewNullability` throws
   `Goal-review nullable patch expected 1 maxMemoryBytes fields, found 2`. The build's own post-patch
   counts **2** `maxMemoryBytes` fields where it asserts **1**, so the post-patch throws and **no**
   post-patch runs at all (both `Bun.write` calls for the later patches sit *after* that point), which
   is why the on-disk output was raw. **Owner:** the owner of `packages/sdk/js/script/build.ts` (the
   SDK build/post-patch pipeline) — **not** P1, and **not** the `packages/schema/src/skill.ts` owner of
   item 1. **Not fixed here** (explicitly out of scope for this task). It must be fixed before any
   future `packages/sdk/js && bun run build` can land the 403 in `gen/types.gen.ts`.
