## 44. 2026-10-25 — source-run HostKernel startup failure: Guard allowlist regression fixed; duplicate lease root cause isolated

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED.** This continuation traced the real source-run `serve` failure without provider traffic, LM-018, Cargo, or changing a readiness gate. The initial process boundary failure and the child failure surfaced after it were distinct:

1. **P1 regression, before child creation:** after §39 changed the two trusted control launches to `stderr: "pipe"`, `AppProcess.isSupervisorControlLaunch` still required `stderr: "ignore"`. A real source-run `serve` reproduced `PermissionDenied: ChildProcess.spawn (Guarded HostKernel capability)` at that Core boundary. No child stderr was possible on this branch because no child had been spawned.
2. **Underlying source-run startup failure, after fixing the boundary:** the same source-run path then reached the real `hz-kernel`; its newly surfaced stderr was `acquire host owner lease: supervisor lease Busy: Resource temporarily unavailable (os error 11)`. A second short-path, fresh repo-contained sandbox reproduced this exact child error. The sandbox's durable epoch advanced to `1`, showing one child acquired the process-held lease before the conflicting acquisition; the second child exited before binding its own socket. This is not an insecure-directory or missing-binary failure.

### Test-first P1 correction

- Added `process-guard.test.ts` regression `allows a manifest-pinned HostKernel control launch to pipe stderr for startup diagnostics`. It creates and manifest-pins an executable inside the test directory, uses the production strict five-argument HostKernel shape, and asserts the trusted command reaches the configured spawner with `stderr: "pipe"`; a test-owned intercepted spawner prevents any child launch.
- **RED:** before production change, the focused test failed at `process.ts:531` with the expected `PermissionDenied: ChildProcess.spawn (Guarded HostKernel capability)`.
- **GREEN:** `isSupervisorControlLaunch` now admits `stderr: "pipe"` in addition to `"ignore"`, while retaining all existing pin/digest/inode, command, argv, stdin/stdout, `extendEnv:false`, and source-scope checks. No generic process or model/tool command is admitted.
- Focused rerun after replacing the real child with an intercepted spawner: `cd packages/core && TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-supervisor-mock" bun test test/process-guard.test.ts --test-name-pattern 'pipe stderr for startup diagnostics'` → **1/1, 3 expectations**.
- Combined regression group before the final test-only mock simplification: `cd packages/core && TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-supervisor-final" bun test test/process-guard.test.ts test/session-supervisor-launch.test.ts` → **24/24, 72 expectations**. On the final test source, the intercepted-spawner regression reran **1/1, 3 expectations** (`.tmp/p1-supervisor-mock-final`) and the launch suite reran **18/18, 53 expectations** (`.tmp/p1-supervisor-launch-final`).

### Source-backed duplicate HostKernel acquisition

The `Busy` result was obtained in a newly created, private repository-local XDG state tree, with no earlier HostKernel invocation using that tree. Current source shows the same `SessionSupervisor.node` is transitively compiled in separate server route graphs:

- `packages/opencode/src/server/routes/instance/httpapi/server.ts:331-335` separately calls `AppNodeBuilderV1.build(SessionV2.node, ...)`; `packages/core/src/session.ts:583-595` lists `SessionSupervisor.node` as a dependency of `SessionV2.node`.
- The same route file separately calls `AppNodeBuilderV1.build(app, ...)` at `:339`; the app group itself also lists `SessionSupervisor.node` at `:253-255`.
- `packages/core/src/effect/layer-node.ts:250-270` creates a new compilation-local cache for each `compile()` and wraps dependency-bearing implementations with a new `Layer.provide(...)` layer. Therefore same-node identity does not deduplicate the supervisor layer across these separate compilations. Since `SessionSupervisor.layer` eagerly opens one host-wide lease in `supervisor.ts:4635-4675`, both graph builds can spawn `hz-kernel` against the same `Global.Path.state/hz-kernel`; one acquires the lease and the other correctly fails `Busy` before socket bind.

This explains the child stderr and is consistent with the fresh-tree epoch evidence. The process-allowlist fix removes the first P1 blocker but does **not** solve duplicate HostKernel ownership. The narrow composition fix belongs at the source-run server/app graph assembly boundary (`packages/opencode/src/server/routes/instance/httpapi/server.ts`), which is outside this P1 write grant; no P2/P9 or server-route file was edited. Parent must assign that route-graph correction to its owning phase or explicitly grant the appropriate shared boundary. Keep HostKernel's exclusive lease, Guard and UNKNOWN semantics unchanged; do not retry around `Busy` or start a second supervisor.

### Source-run evidence and remaining gates

- Real source run, using `.p1-serve-diagnostic` under the repository and the exact source bootstrap `.p6-e2e/bootstrap.ts`, first produced the process-boundary denial above. After the P1 fix, it surfaced the child's `SupervisorLeaseErrorKind::Busy` and did not bind the reported child socket. A second run from fresh `.p1d` state repeated `Busy` with a short socket path. No provider request or LM-018 turn was initiated.
- A separate fixed-port `serve` attempt with `.p1s` remained alive until the bounded `timeout` (exit 124) but did not produce a HostKernel state tree or a listening confirmation; it is not counted as startup acceptance.
- **Filesystem-boundary incident:** the first GREEN version of the regression used a test-local executable with `#!/bin/sh`; that version was run once before noticing the interpreter crossed the repository boundary. The test was immediately changed to an intercepted spawner and the final 24-test group above was rerun with no child launched by the regression. No outside file was read or modified; no further such test execution was performed.
- The parent-owned `run` wrapper's `Session not found` still discards the underlying cause (`packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/run/runtime.ts:757-759`, `cmd/run.ts:461-466`). It is a separate diagnostic defect/owner, and no P9-owned file was changed. With the current source evidence, `Session not found` is downstream and is not the HostKernel root cause.
- No runtime gate changed; `GUARDED_PTY_RUNTIME_READY` and `GUARDED_TERMINAL_RUN_READY` remain false. No provider/network, credential, LM-018, Cargo, full typecheck, Git operation, or publication occurred. No dependent capability is newly unblocked.
- P1 still has open B4 delegated-cgroup/mount-namespace/process-tree evidence (unsupported here), integrated Guard→lease→receipt and cancellation/restart UNKNOWN/no-replay acceptance, P1-10 Rust/P7 streaming integration, P1-11 parent-owned measurement, plus P2's stable HostRun binding/projection and P8's authenticated evidence prerequisite. Existing P1 Rust acceptance evidence remains as previously recorded; this continuation did not run Cargo because P2 owns the slot.

No persistent product process is claimed. P1 remains **PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED**.

## 45. 2026-10-25 — source-run `serve` duplicate host-owner lease: root cause traced and fixed (test-first)

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED.** §44 isolated the `supervisor lease Busy`
child failure but left the composition defect outside P1's grant. This continuation traced it to the
exact memo-map boundary, was granted `packages/opencode/src/server/**` for the fix, and proved the
repair with a red→green unit pair, the full `test/server` suite against a no-fix baseline, and three
real source-run `hz-kernel` journeys. No Cargo, provider/network request, credential, LM-018, Git
operation, package-wide typecheck, or readiness gate was touched; `GUARDED_PTY_RUNTIME_READY` and
`GUARDED_TERMINAL_RUN_READY` remain `false`.

### 45.1 Root cause (evidence, not inference)

Two facts fix the diagnosis:

1. **Log ordering.** In the failing run the handler's `Warning: OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD is not
   set…` is printed *before* `Server.listen` is reached, and `effectCmd` runs `serve` through
   `AppRuntime.runPromise` — so `ManagedRuntime.make(AppLayer, { memoMap })` had already built
   `AppLayer` (which lists `SessionSupervisor.node`, `packages/opencode/src/effect/app-runtime.ts:97`)
   and its `hz-kernel` child had taken the lease (`owner-epoch` = `1`). The death was the *second*
   construction, inside `startListener`.
2. **Control experiment (fresh repo-contained XDG tree, same `.p6-e2e/bootstrap.ts` globals):**
   `Server.listen` **without** AppRuntime starts, serves and stops cleanly —
   `LISTENING http://127.0.0.1:4096/`, `DOC_STATUS 200`, `STOPPED`, `owner-epoch = 1`. The identical
   listener build inside `serve` fails with `supervisor lease Busy`. So the conflict is exactly two
   builds of `SessionSupervisor.layer` in one process, not an insecure directory, a bad binary, or a
   route-graph defect.

The mechanism is Effect's memo map: `MemoMapImpl` is keyed by **layer object identity**
(`Layer.js` `memoMapBuild`/`getOrElseMemoize`), and `Layer.effect`/`Layer.sync` build through
`fromBuildMemo`. `startListener` called
`Layer.buildWithMemoMap(listenerLayer(...), Layer.makeMemoMapUnsafe(), scope)` — a **fresh map per
attempt** — while AppRuntime and the lazy `webHandler` use the process-wide
`@opencode-ai/core/effect/memo-map`. A private map cannot see the supervisor AppRuntime already
owns, so it constructed a second one, and the second `hz-kernel` correctly refused the held lease.

**§44's stated hypothesis is corrected:** the three separate `AppNodeBuilderV1.build(...)` calls
inside `createRoutes()` are *not* the duplication. Because they all compile inside one
`buildWithMemoMap`, they memoize on the same `SessionSupervisor.node.implementation` object — proven
by the control run above, which builds all three and acquires exactly one lease. The duplicate is
between two different memo maps, not between two compilations.

### 45.2 First attempt and why it was rejected (kept the route graph private)

The one-line fix — `startListener` building with the process-wide `memoMap` — turned the suite red:
`test/server/httpapi-cors.test.ts` lost custom CORS origins whenever `httpapi-config.test.ts`
(which uses `Server.Default()`) ran first. Mechanism: the route tree's module-level layers
(`rootApiRoutes`, `instanceRoutes`, the auth layers, …) are memoized too, so a listener *reused*
another build's route tree and its `cors(corsOptions)` / `ServerAuth.Config` / per-listener
`ConfigProvider` providers were never applied to it. Reproduced deterministically
(`httpapi-config` + `httpapi-cors` → 1 fail with that variant, 0 fail without it) and abandoned.

The correct seam is narrower: **share the app-service graph only, keep each listener's route tree
private.**

### 45.3 Fix (P1-owned files)

- `packages/opencode/src/server/routes/instance/httpapi/server.ts` — `createRoutes` body factored
  into `routeTree(corsOptions, replacements, shareServices)`; new exported
  `createListenerRoutes(...)` passes `shareServices: true`. `share(layer)` is
  `Layer.fromBuild((_, scope) => Layer.buildWithMemoMap(layer, memoMap, scope))`, applied to exactly
  the three service builds that reach `SessionSupervisor.node` (`MoveSession`, `SessionV2`, the
  `app` group). Their in-graph builds therefore run in the process map, while their ambient
  requirements still come from the same deeper providers as before. `createRoutes` (used by
  `webHandler`, `routes` and existing tests) is byte-for-byte the previous behavior
  (`shareServices: false`).
- `packages/opencode/src/server/server.ts` — `listenerLayer` now serves
  `HttpApiApp.createListenerRoutes(opts)`; `startListener` keeps its private map (with a comment
  recording both halves of the contract), so per-attempt cors/auth/ConfigProvider and the
  port-fallback scope release are unchanged.
- `.gitignore` — one `!/packages/opencode/test/server/listener-memo-map.test.ts` line so the new
  focused test is Git-visible under the overlay model (`audit_overlay_visibility.py` →
  `hidden_new_source=0`, exit 0).
- `packages/opencode/test/server/listener-memo-map.test.ts` — new focused regression test (below).

No lease, Guard, receipt or readiness semantics were changed: exclusivity still belongs to
`hz-kernel`, no retry-around-`Busy` exists, and a second supervisor is no longer constructed at all.

### 45.4 Test-first evidence (exact commands and results)

From `packages/opencode`, repository-local `TMPDIR`:

```sh
# RED — before the fix (listener still calling createRoutes)
bun test test/server/listener-memo-map.test.ts
#   (fail) listener layer memoization > a listener shares the process-wide service graph …
#          error: SessionSupervisor must be memoized process-wide  (Expected true, Received false)
#    2 pass  1 fail  10 expect() calls
# RED, run alone so no earlier afterEach has built AppRuntime:
bun test test/server/listener-memo-map.test.ts -t "failed port attempt"
#    0 pass  1 fail  3 expect() calls
# GREEN — after the fix
bun test test/server/listener-memo-map.test.ts
#    3 pass  0 fail  13 expect() calls
```

The three cases: (1) a live listener must show `SessionSupervisor` memoized **process-wide** while
`WebSocketTracker.node` — built only by `listenerLayer` — stays **out** of the process map (the
route tree is private); both return to their pre-listen state after `stop(true)`; (2) the same
invariants across a real `port 0` → 4096-occupied → fallback attempt; (3) a service build routed
through the process map is constructed **once across two private outer builds**, while the same
graph built directly in a private map constructs again (the pre-fix defect, and for
`SessionSupervisor` a second `hz-kernel` competing for the lease).

Regression, short repo-local `TMPDIR` (the long path from §42 reintroduces the known
`Unix socket path is too long` noise):

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `bun test test/server` **with no fix** (baseline) | 313 pass / 9 skip / **35 fail** (33 pre-existing + my 2 red tests) |
| `bun test test/server` **with the fix** (run E2) | 316 pass / 9 skip / **32 fail** — the failure set is a strict **subset** of the baseline (no new failures; `listener memoization` green, `HttpApi CORS` green) |
| `bun test test/server/listener-memo-map.test.ts httpapi-config.test.ts httpapi-cors.test.ts httpapi-listen.test.ts` | 19 pass / 3 skip / **0 fail** |
| `bun test test/server/httpapi-listen.test.ts` | 11 pass / 3 skip / 0 fail (port-0 preference, 4096-occupied fallback, stop/restart, `Server.Default`) |
| `bunx tsgo --noEmit -p tsconfig.p1-lease.json` (temp config over the 3 changed files) | **0 diagnostics in P1-owned files**; the single remaining diagnostic is pre-existing in `src/image/image.ts` (other owner). Config deleted after use; no package-wide typecheck run. |

One earlier full-suite run (under load, 240 s vs 173 s) showed 4 extra `provider`/`initGit`/
`schema-rejection` failures; they did not reproduce in a second identical run nor in isolation
(`bun test` on those three files: 14 pass / 1 fail-skip / 0 fail), so they are recorded as
load-induced flake, not attributed to this change.

### 45.5 Real `hz-kernel` journeys (source run, repo-contained, no network)

All three used `.p6-e2e/bootstrap.ts`-equivalent globals with `env -i` and fresh
`HOME/XDG_*/TMPDIR` trees inside the repository; binaries are P2's existing
`target-p2/debug/hz-kernel` (no Cargo command was run).

| Journey | Result |
|---|---|
| RED before fix: `bun .p6-e2e/bootstrap.ts serve --port 0` | exit 1 — `supervisor exited before binding its socket …; exit code 1; stderr: hz-kernel: acquire host owner lease: supervisor lease Busy: Resource temporarily unavailable (os error 11)`, `owner-epoch = 1` |
| Control: `Server.listen` only (no AppRuntime) | `LISTENING …/4096`, `DOC_STATUS 200`, `STOPPED`, `owner-epoch = 1` → the three-route-build listener holds **one** lease |
| GREEN: `serve --port 4175` with the fix | `opencode server listening on http://127.0.0.1:4175`, `owner-epoch = 1`, **exactly 1** `hz-kernel` process, `GET /status` → **200** |
| Release proof: listen → `stop(true)` → listen again, same process | `FIRST … epoch=1` → `FIRST_STOPPED` → `SECOND … epoch=2` → `SECOND_DOC 200`. The kernel only advances the epoch when it *acquires* the lease, so `1 → 2` is direct proof that closing a listener attempt's scope stops the child and releases the lease before a later build. |

Port-fallback release detail: with `port 0` and 4096 occupied, the first attempt fails at bind
*before* the route graph (and therefore the supervisor) is built — `serverLayer` is provided
beneath the route chain — so that attempt never owns the lease; the fallback attempt acquires it
once. The release-before-retry ordering still holds structurally for any attempt that does own it:
`startListener`'s `Effect.onError(() => Scope.close(scope, …))` completes before
`startWithPortFallback`'s `Effect.catch` starts the next attempt, `Scope.close` awaits the layer
finalizers, and `HostKernelClientImpl.stop()` awaits child exit (`supervisor.ts:4493`) before
resolving.

- B4 delegated-cgroup/mount-namespace/process-tree matrix: **NOT RUN / unsupported here**; unit
  negatives are not containment proof. Both terminal readiness gates stay `false`.
- P1 Rust acceptance wave, P1-10 (P2 RPC + P7 dispatch), P1-11 parent-owned measurement, P2's stable
  HostRun binding/projection, P8's authenticated receipt fixture.
- Integrated grant/profile/terminal Guard→lease→receipt, final executable revalidation,
  cancellation/restart UNKNOWN/no-replay and live Core→HostKernel acceptance remain **NOT VERIFIED**.
- The parent-owned `run` wrapper still discards the cause of `Session not found`
  (`packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/run/runtime.ts:757-759`, `cmd/run.ts:461-466`, owner P9) — with
  this fix it is the only remaining diagnostic defect on that path, not a lease issue.

## 47. 2026-10-25 — bind Core Bash validation to the Guarded terminal call

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED.** Fixed an independently runnable P1 Core
Guard-boundary gap without changing any readiness gate. `Tool.settle` schema-decodes the model's Bash
input, while the leaf dispatches the separately supplied `context.guardedCall.arguments`; the leaf
previously checked only call ID/capability plus lease/Location presence. A stale or mismatched
private context could therefore pass one command to the model-facing validation and a different
command to `Pty.runTool`. The live runner normally derives both from one call, but this leaf is the
last Core boundary before terminal dispatch and must bind them explicitly.

### Test-first evidence

- Added a regression that provides a schema-valid `true` Bash call but a same-ID `terminal.run`
  Guarded call containing `rm -rf /workspace`; it requires denial before the terminal runner is
  invoked. The positive fixture now represents the canonical six-key shell projection
  (`mode`, command, cwd, timeout, trusted shell profile, resolved environment).

## 49. 2026-10-25 — classify current HostRun process/effect projection contract

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED.** Re-read the current source instead of
relying on the older §40/§48 descriptions. The typed HostRun projection contract is now source-present
in P2-owned `kernel/src/managed_control.rs`; P1 must not recreate it in another module or change that
file under this continuation's scope. Its presence is not evidence that the production HostKernel
route can use it or that terminal projection acceptance passes.

### Exact boundary and ownership

- P2 constructs `HostRunProcessEffectProjectionRequestV1` only from the durable
  `HostRunDispatchBindingV1`, a committed managed effect wave, and its committed receipt map via
  `for_committed_wave` (`managed_control.rs:2974-3113`). The request pins schema/binding/dispatch-key
  digests; batch, Run, Task, Attempt, Session and principal identities; owner epoch, Core process
  generation, per-wave Attempt-scope digest; and ordered call/effect/capability/arguments/intent/lease
  digests. It refuses a missing or UNKNOWN committed edge receipt, a receipt identity mismatch,
  absent/malformed scope digest, mixed per-wave scope digests, or malformed lease digest. This is a
  HostKernel-derived read selector, not caller-supplied authority or outcome evidence.
- The P1 response is `HostRunProcessEffectProjectionV1` plus ordered members
  (`managed_control.rs:3115-3191`). It echoes every request identity/pin and reports effect outcome,
  optional terminal receipt ref/digest, and process quiescence `PROVED | UNKNOWN`. `validate_against`
  checks the exact request echo and member ordering; `is_complete_terminal` requires settled status,
  proved quiescence, and no UNKNOWN member.
- `HostRunEffectProjectionSourceV1::project_committed_batch` is the P1 read-only provider seam
  (`managed_control.rs:3341-3350`). The persisted implementation
  `HostRunPersistedEffectProjectionSourceV1` reads effect journals read-only and joins committed
  effect/decision rows with process/quiescence rows (`managed_control.rs:4050-4718`). Missing,
  duplicate, substituted, stale, ambiguous, nonterminal, or unquiesced facts must return a typed
  conflict or an explicit UNKNOWN projection; no dispatch, retry, receipt creation, or authority
  minting belongs to this provider.
- The provider trait and installer method are present, but current source search finds no call to
  `install_host_run_effect_projection_source` or construction of the provider in HostKernel startup.
  `managed_host_run_inspect` therefore retains its explicit unavailable→UNKNOWN branch until P2
  wires the source (`host_kernel.rs:15815-15915`). This is the remaining production install/wire
  dependency, not a missing P1 request/response type.
- P8 still owns the authenticated post-commit OwnerLog receipt and evaluator positive fixture; the
  P1 projection cannot derive dispatch binding or P8 cursor/event digest. P2 owns binding, inspect
  caller/install and managed terminal/verifier joins. P1 owns only the persisted EffectHost/process
  evidence provider and its cross-boundary verification. Do not accept P8's independent worker
  settlement receipt as the HostRun binding receipt.

## 55. 2026-10-25 — `apply_patch` contract trace and fail-closed boundary

**Verdict: P1 remains PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED. No adapter was implemented.** Following §54, read only the normative §14 mutation/Guard rules, §33 canonical capability mapping, §37 relevant patch references, and the current Core leaf, registry, runner, Supervisor API, and tests.

### Contract trace

- **§14 is normative about authority but not the patch-text adapter.** §14.2 requires resource-owner resolution before Guard, repeated canonical path enforcement at open/dispatch, and ALLOW bound to exact action/arguments/resources/revision/principal/policy/location/expiry. §14.3 requires complete batch admission, PREPARED, one-use lease, ExecutionHost, receipt or UNKNOWN, and no blind replay. §14.26's language-service text edits must return through ordinary `files.patch`, and stale document/workspace preimages deny. These rules establish the required safety properties, not the OpenCode `patchText` compiler or its read/preimage authorization sequence.
- **§33 supplies the ID and mapping, not an input DTO.** §33.5 lists `files.patch` as an effect and maps model tool `apply_patch` to that capability; the note points to a hashline/revision fence in §10.5/§30.29. No `patchText`→operation mapping, preimage acquisition rule, receipt schema, or UNKNOWN projection is defined in §33.5 itself.
- **§37 specifies the boundary, not a patch service interface.** §37.6 says language-service edits apply only through `files.patch`/Guard; its LSP text edits remain proposals and accepted changes re-enter through versioned `files.patch`, where stale preimages deny. It does not define a callable Core patch adapter, request DTO, or conversion algorithm.
- **Current canonical Core/Rust seam is operations-only.** `SessionSupervisor.PatchOperation` is `{operation: "write"|"delete", path, expectedBeforeBase64, contentBase64?, executable?}`. `validatePatchOperations` bounds count/bytes, requires unique paths and exact write/delete shapes. `prepareToolBatch` accepts `files.patch` only when `arguments.operations` passes that validator. `executePatch` forwards the exact operations with Location, lease, principal and returns a receipt whose effect ID/digest/targets are checked. `runPatch` composes prepare→one-use lease→execute→target-receipt validation, but it accepts already-formed operations plus `workspaceRoot` and `turnContextDigest`; no Core caller currently uses it.
- **Current OpenCode/Core path cannot produce those operations at the Guard boundary.** `apply_patch` takes only `{patchText}`, parses add/update/delete hunks, and its old leaf reads and mutates using `FSUtil`/`FileMutation`. It has no declared capability, lease, principal, `SessionSupervisor`, or Rust receipt. The registry classifies it as `unknown` and excludes it from model definitions. The session runner’s guarded path would submit capability ID `apply_patch` and raw `{patchText}` to `prepareToolBatch`; it cannot submit the required `files.patch` plus `operations` without a compiler and authoritative preimage source. Simply setting the capability would therefore be incorrect and fail-closed at the Supervisor, not authorize the leaf.

**Conclusion:** existing authority does not fully specify conversion, preimage/target binding, or the Core receipt/UNKNOWN result contract. The existing `runPatch`/`executePatch` API can carry canonical operations after they exist, but does not supply or authorize their derivation from model `patchText`. No P2 file or shared Supervisor was changed.

### Narrow negative regression

Added one test to `packages/core/test/tool-apply-patch.test.ts` asserting current fail-closed behavior for both valid and malformed patch text: `apply_patch` is absent from materialized definitions, settlement returns `Unknown tool: apply_patch`, PermissionV2 receives no assertion, no pre-approval content read occurs, and the workspace fixture remains unchanged. The test is explicitly an availability-boundary test, not patch acceptance.

| Exact command (from `packages/core`, repository-contained TMPDIR) | Result |
|---|---:|
| `TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-core-test-tmp" bun test test/tool-apply-patch.test.ts -t "keeps patchText unavailable"` | **1 passed / 9 filtered / 0 failed; 7 expectations** |

The unfiltered legacy suite had already reported **0 passed / 9 failed** before this new boundary case, because its old cases expect execution of the unguarded local leaf; this continuation did not rewrite those expected-success tests or claim patch availability. No Cargo, HostKernel, provider/network, live product effect, Git mutation, readiness-gate change, P2 edit, or LEDGER edit occurred.

### Exact decision/interface needed to resume adapter work

P1 needs an explicit architecture/owner decision before implementing the adapter:

1. **Canonical compiler input and Guard subject:** Is the approved action the exact raw `patchText`, or a deterministic bounded canonical operation list? If operations, define one OpenCode-hunk→`PatchOperation[]` compiler and its normalization/fuzzy-match rules, including add/delete/update, executable state, BOM, duplicates, limits, and rejected move semantics.
2. **Preimage and target authority:** Specify which trusted owner reads source bytes and resolves paths before Guard, how that read itself is authorized (especially external directories), and how its revision/hashline is carried. Define the exact check at execution for symlink/target substitution and stale preimage. Do not treat `expectedBeforeBase64` alone as authority before the Guard input has been bound to it.
3. **Result/recovery contract:** Define how Core receives a typed settled receipt versus denial, known partial application, cancellation, and UNKNOWN; how it fences later batch members and prevents automatic reapplication after a possibly dispatched patch. Align the response with the current target receipt and durable Rust reconciliation owner.
4. **Ownership/interface allocation:** Confirm whether P1 owns only the Core compiler/caller adapter over existing `operations` RPCs, or whether P2 must extend admission/receipt projection. If admission stays P2-owned, P2’s exact accepted DTO must be documented before P1 wires Core. Any changed shared Supervisor contract needs explicit file-owner arbitration.

Until those are settled in the normative owner/interface, keep `apply_patch` unavailable. No cross-phase dependency became satisfied; all other P1 rows in §54 remain open. Both PTY readiness flags remain false; P1 remains **PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED**.

## 57. 2026-10-26 — cap streamed host-digest probe output before line splitting

**Verdict: PHASE_PARTIAL / NOT VERIFIED / NOT RELEASED.** Continued P1 in the
Core process boundary without changing readiness or crossing into P2/P7/P8/P9
ownership. The previous hard cap applied to `run` collection, but `runStream`
passed the pinned host-digest probe's byte stream directly to `decodeText` and
`splitLines`. A single oversized line could therefore accumulate as an
unbounded string despite the fixed cap in `run`.

### Test-first change and verification

- Added a real pinned-probe regression that writes one 1,000-byte line and
  asserts the streamed result is limited to the fixed 128-byte budget. It
  checks both stdout-only and combined stdout/stderr (`includeStderr`) streams.
- **RED:** `bun test test/process-guard.test.ts --test-name-pattern 'hard-bounds streamed output'`
  → **0 pass / 1 fail**; received 1,000 characters instead of 128.
- `packages/core/src/process.ts` now caps each `Uint8Array` chunk cumulatively
  before text decoding and line splitting. This bounds line accumulation for
  both stdout and the explicitly combined `includeStderr` stream while keeping
  the underlying pipe drained; `runStream` remains admitted only for the
  manifest-pinned host-digest probe. The existing `maxErrorBytes` path uses the
  same finite-cap normalizer.
- Expanded the cap-normalization regression to verify NaN→0, negative→0, and
  fractional→truncated integer behavior. Positive infinity remains covered by
  the existing hard-bound `run` test.
- **GREEN:** from `packages/core`, `bun test test/process-guard.test.ts` →
  **10 passed / 0 failed / 31 expectations**.

No Cargo, readiness/gate change, provider/network request, application effect,
P2 HostKernel/managed-control, parent QA-007, P7/P8/P9 or LEDGER file was
touched. The test's pinned executable fixture ran only inside the repository

## 58. 2026-10-26 — make the stream collector itself fail bounded

Continued P1 with a direct `collectStream` seam regression. Although `run` and
`runStream` now normalize caller limits, the exported collector still treated
`undefined` as unlimited and passed `NaN` through to byte accounting. A direct
or future caller could therefore bypass the wrapper-level invariant.

- Added a test using one 2,048-byte in-memory chunk. It requires NaN to produce
  a zero-byte truncated result and an omitted limit to stop at the collector's
  fixed 1,024-byte ceiling.
- **RED:** from `packages/core`, `bun test test/process-guard.test.ts
  --test-name-pattern 'collectStream keeps malformed'` → **0 pass / 1 fail**;
  NaN output was empty but incorrectly reported `truncated: false`.
- `collectStream` now normalizes its own requested limit to a finite integer in
  `[0, 1024]`, using 1,024 when omitted; it no longer has an unlimited branch.
  Wrapper paths may still choose their lower limits (128 for the digest probe,
  1,024 for stderr).
- **GREEN:** `bun test test/process-guard.test.ts` → **11 passed / 0 failed / 35
  expectations**, including the earlier single-line stdout and combined
  stdout/stderr stream caps and cap normalization coverage.

## 61. 2026-10-26 — observer startup lifecycle source trace (no runtime inference)

Continued the P1-06 investigation from the exact Core source without rerunning
the timed-out process or changing startup behavior. The test calls
`openForSession` at `packages/core/test/kernel-projection-live.test.ts:218`;
before it can reach the cursor/ACK assertions, the supervisor lifecycle includes
these source-defined boundaries:

1. During `SessionSupervisor` layer evaluation,
   `packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts:4637-4675` parses the optional
   HostKernel manifest, installs its binary, runs the host-digest probe,
   installs the CodeMode helper and calls `openHostKernel`.
2. `openHostKernel` (`:3960-4007`) validates its state/socket paths, spawns
   HostKernel, then awaits `establishHostChannel`. That function (`:3910-3944`)
   waits up to 10 seconds for the socket and up to 10 seconds to connect, then
   awaits the Hello reply and startup-status frames.
3. `openForSession` (`:4779-4830`) validates the Session/source and observer
   manifest, installs the observer binary, creates the per-session state
   directory, computes the digest, and calls `open`.
4. `open` (`:4679-4777`) validates the active Core DB and state/socket paths,
   requires the HostKernel owner epoch, spawns `session_supervisor`, then awaits
   `establishChannel`. This uses the same 10-second socket/connect bounds and
   then awaits Hello and READY status.

**Observed:** the earlier Bun test timed out after its path assertions, with the
await at `openForSession` not completing; cursor/ACK/replay assertions were not
reached. **Source fact:** the socket wait/connect helpers have deadlines, but
the subsequent `MessageChannel.receive()` calls for Hello and startup status in
both handshakes have no local timeout in these call sites. **Not established:**
which layer boundary (HostKernel setup, observer setup, or either handshake)
accounted for the test timeout. There is no stage-specific log from that run,
and this source trace cannot identify the stalled await. No production
workaround, timeout change, or lifecycle reordering is justified by this
evidence; P1-06 remains NOT VERIFIED pending stage-specific evidence.

## 63. 2026-10-26 — bound CodeMode helper asset pinning

Continued the independent P1 process-authorization review after §62. The
bundled helper installer already rejects empty assets and assets larger than
16 MiB, but the exported `pinCodeModeHelperRoot` hashed the file without
checking the securely inspected file size. A direct caller could therefore
make the pin boundary read/hash an asset the trusted installer would never
accept.

- **RED:** from `packages/core`,
  `bun test test/process-guard.test.ts --test-name-pattern 'CodeMode helper
  asset larger'` → **0 passed / 1 failed**; a validly hashed 16 MiB + 1 byte
  asset was pinned rather than rejected.
- `pinCodeModeHelperRoot` now rejects empty helper assets and assets over 16 MiB
  using the existing secure file metadata, before reading or hashing. This
  matches the already-enforced `installBundledCodeModeHelper` bounds and does
  not alter the trusted asset contract.
- **GREEN:** `bun test test/process-guard.test.ts` → **13 passed / 0 failed / 39
  expectations**, including §62's manifest bound and the prior collector and
  executable pinning regressions.

`packages/core/test/process-guard.test.ts`, and this report. No Cargo, P2/P8

## 68. 2026-10-26 — P1-10 caller pre-dispatch fixes and CodeMode context binding

Continued P1 items 11–20 without Cargo or product effects. The direct read-batch
caller and CodeMode authenticated-context validator each had one concrete local
fail-closed gap. Both were fixed test-first.

### P1-10 direct read-batch caller

`runWave` classified a direct-read wave, but it only checked the canonical
`requestID` later inside `prepare`; a malformed request identity therefore
reached `hostFeatureEnabled` and `registerHostLocation` before denial. A
4,096-byte canonical path also passed local validation but exceeded the
arguments-digest encoder's 4,096-byte bound, causing a thrown `RangeError`
instead of a typed whole-wave failure.

- **RED (malformed request identity):** from `packages/core`,
  `TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-current" bun test
  test/session-direct-read-batch.test.ts --test-name-pattern 'rejects a malformed request identity'`
  → **0 passed / 1 failed; 4 expectations**. The log showed
  `feature:effects.v2` and `register` before denial.
- **RED (maximum-length path):** the focused maximum-length-path test threw
  `RangeError: canonical MessagePack value exceeds its encoded byte bound` at
  `directReadArgumentsDigestV1`.
- `packages/core/src/session/direct-read-batch.ts` now validates the request
  identity immediately after direct-wave classification, before any feature
  probe, Location registration, send, or execution, and bounds the arguments
  digest encoder at 4,224 bytes so the existing 4 KiB path bound plus map/page
  envelope cannot throw during digest recomputation.
- **GREEN:** the focused malformed-identity test passed **1/1**, the focused
  maximum-length-path test passed **1/1**, and the full changed file passed
  `bun test test/session-direct-read-batch.test.ts` → **26 passed / 0 failed; 458
  expectations**.

### CodeMode authenticated-context binding

`validCodeModeAuthenticatedRequest` checked the host feature context's lane and
Attempt parity but did not bind the RPC body's `sessionID`, `locationID`, or
`callID` to the authenticated context. A direct-session CodeMode request could
therefore carry a foreign call identity/location while still passing Core's
pre-send gate.

- Added `packages/core/test/session-supervisor-code-mode-context.test.ts` with a
  fake `HostKernelTransport`. It asserts direct-session and managed-attempt
  bodies must echo the exact authenticated Session/Location/call and, for
  managed attempts, the exact Run/Task/Attempt; malformed ServerAuth subjects,
  host-OS principals and malformed call identities fail before any frame.
- **RED:** before the validator change, substituted call IDs, Locations and
  Session IDs resolved through the fake transport instead of rejecting.
- `packages/core/src/session/supervisor.ts` now requires exact body
  `sessionID`/`locationID`/`callID` equality before the existing lane/Attempt
  checks.
- **GREEN:** `bun test test/session-supervisor-code-mode-context.test.ts` →
  **3 passed / 0 failed; 20 expectations**.

### Additional independent terminal-profile coverage

Added to `packages/core/test/tool-bash.test.ts`: an exact workspace-profile
Guard admission passes only the admitted profile reference to the terminal
runner; substituted profile revision/digest/action/argv are rejected before the
runner. Focused command passed **2/2**; full changed file passed **6 passed / 0
failed; 23 expectations**.

test/session-supervisor-code-mode-context.test.ts test/tool-bash.test.ts

## 72. 2026-10-26 — delegated-cgroup/mount-namespace product-matrix attempt

Attempted item 13 locally under the authorized honest-attempt rules: no sudo,
no writes outside the repository, no unrelated process inspection or killing,
and every spawned process task-owned and short-lived.

### OS capability probes

Commands captured to `kernel/tmp-p1/cgroup-ns-probe.log`:

- `command -v bwrap` → `/usr/bin/bwrap`
- `bwrap --version` → `bubblewrap 0.9.0`
- `unshare -U -m true` → exit 0
- `unshare -U -C true` → exit 0
- `/proc/self/cgroup` → `0::/non-systemd`
- `/sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd` stat → `mode=755 uid=0 gid=0`
- `test -w /sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd` → `current_cgroup_writable=no`

Control-file read-only checks for `/sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd`:

- `cgroup.controllers` mode 444, readable, not writable
- `cgroup.subtree_control` mode 644, readable, not writable
- `cgroup.procs` mode 644, readable, not writable
- `cgroup.events` mode 444, readable, not writable
- `cgroup.kill` mode 200, not readable, not writable

### Repo-local bubblewrap mount-scope probe

A task-owned fixture under `kernel/.tmp/p1-cgroup-ns-probe` was created with
mode 700. The bwrap command used `--die-with-parent --unshare-user --unshare-pid
--unshare-net --unshare-cgroup --ro-bind / / --proc /proc --dev /dev`, bound the
fixture workspace over `/tmp`, and ran only task-owned `/bin/sh` probes.

Result captured to `kernel/tmp-p1/bwrap-scope-probe.log`:

```text
inside_rc=0
outside_rc=0
cgroup_info=0::/
cgroup_mount_mode=755 uid=65534 gid=65534
cgroup_mount_writable=no
inside_marker=yes
inside_marker_2=yes
outside_host_marker=no
outside_err=touch: cannot touch '/outside-marker': Read-only file system
```

Interpretation:
- The unprivileged user/mount/PID/network/cgroup namespaces are available.
- A task-owned process can write only inside the bound fixture workspace.
- A write to the host-visible outside marker is denied `EROFS`.
- Inside a cgroup namespace, the cgroup2 root is mounted with uid/gid 65534 and
  mode 755, so it is not writable by this principal.

### Product-matrix conclusion

Item 13 is **EVIDENCED-UNAVAILABLE on this host**, not unknown. The product’s
`ExecutionCgroupV2::create` requires the current process’s cgroup root to be
owned by the ExecutionHost principal and to provide writable
`cgroup.procs`/`cgroup.kill`; this host’s `/sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd` is
root-owned and non-writable. No system cgroup write was attempted because it
would require a write outside the repository and the OS does not provide a
repo-local delegated cgroup subtree. This is a platform blocker, not a P1
production defect. Both readiness flags remain false.

### Cleanup

- `kernel/.tmp/p1-cgroup-ns-probe` removed.
- `/outside-marker` was never created on the host.
- Temporary probe script removed; evidence logs remain under `kernel/tmp-p1/`.

## 74. 2026-10-26 — Core duplicate-column migration reconciliation

Parent routed the pre-existing `packages/server/test/session-revert-authorization.test.ts`
failure: **0 pass / 7 fail**, every case dying during DB setup with
`SQLiteError: duplicate column name: delivery_prompt_message_id` at
`packages/core/src/database/sqlite.bun.ts`.

### Root-cause ruling

The failure is a migration-journal replay conflict, not an auth assertion and
not a P6/P7 defect:

- `packages/core/src/database/schema.gen.ts` already creates
  `managed_subagent_inbox` with `delivery_prompt_message_id`.
- `2026102110000_managed_subagent_inbox` creates the table without the delivery
  columns.
- `20261021110000_managed_subagent_consumption` then unconditionally runs
  `ALTER TABLE managed_subagent_inbox ADD COLUMN delivery_prompt_message_id`.
- `20261025022602_cheerful_gamma_corps` also creates the table with the delivery
  column.

On a partially journaled existing database, the table can already contain the
delivery column while `20261021110000_managed_subagent_consumption` is not
recorded in the `migration` journal. `DatabaseMigration.applyOnly` then replays
the ALTER and SQLite rejects it with the duplicate-column error. The P6 delta
control showed zero delta, so this was pre-existing.

### RED

Added a controlled partial-journal regression to
`packages/core/test/database-migration.test.ts`:

`replaying managed subagent consumption on a schema-complete table is idempotent`

It applies the full migration set, deletes only the
`20261021110000_managed_subagent_consumption` journal row, then replays that
migration.

RED command:

```text
OPENCODE_DB=:memory: TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-db" bun test test/database-migration.test.ts --test-name-pattern 'replaying managed subagent consumption'
```

Result: **0 passed / 1 failed; 19 filtered out**, with the exact
`SQLiteError: duplicate column name: delivery_prompt_message_id`.

### Fix

`packages/core/src/database/migration/20261021110000_managed_subagent_consumption.ts`
now inspects `pragma_table_info('managed_subagent_inbox')` inside its migration
transaction and adds only columns that are missing. This is forward-safe
migration-history reconciliation: it preserves the intended schema and does not
delete or skip the migration.

### GREEN

Focused regression after the fix:

```text
OPENCODE_DB=:memory: TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-db" bun test test/database-migration.test.ts --test-name-pattern 'replaying managed subagent consumption'
```

→ **1 passed / 0 failed; 19 filtered out**.

Full Core migration suite:

```text
OPENCODE_DB=:memory: TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-db" bun test test/database-migration.test.ts
```

→ **20 passed / 0 failed; 42 expectations**.

Failing server authorization suite:

```text
OPENCODE_DB=:memory: TMPDIR="$PWD/.tmp/p1-revert" bun test test/session-revert-authorization.test.ts
```

→ **7 passed / 0 failed; 45 expectations**. All seven auth/no-mutation
assertions remain intact; none were weakened or skipped.

- `packages/core/test/database-migration.test.ts`
- `reports/phases/P1.md`

### Current Edit, Core process, CodeMode and terminal status

- **Direct Edit now uses the canonical `files.patch` adapter.** `EditTool`
  declares `files.patch`; `prepareEditBatch` confines targets to the workspace,
  captures exact preimage/content bytes, and `SessionTools` sends `{operations}`
  to ToolBatch Guard before dispatch. The executor recomputes under its per-file
  lock, compares the exact guarded operation set, requires a valid one-use lease
  and authenticated `server_auth` principal, calls
  `SessionSupervisor.executePatch`, settles the ToolBatch lease, validates the
  receipt effect/targets, and only then publishes file/LSP events. Direct
  `afs.writeWithDirs`, `ctx.ask`, and post-write `Format.file` are absent. Edit
  no longer invokes a formatter after commit; formatter output must re-enter as
  guarded operations to avoid a local-write bypass.

  repository `.tmp`):
